# 第 41 章：All Good Things（美好的终结）

> **原书**：《Learning Python》（6th Edition），作者 Mark Lutz
> **本章地位**：第41章是本书的最后一章，回顾Python的发展历程，讨论其变化速率，并展望未来。作为收尾章节，它总结全书的核心思想。
---

## Chapter Introduction（章节导言）

### 英文原文

> Chapter 41. 
> All Good Things
> Welcome to the end of the book! Now that you’ve made it this far, this chapter
> says a few words in closing about Python’s evolution before turning you loose
> on the software field and then wraps up with a bit of fun.
> You’ve now had a chance to see the entire Python language yourself—including
> some advanced features that may seem at odds with a scripting language meant
> to be accessible to nonprofessionals. Though many users will understandably
> accept this as status quo, in an open source project, it’s crucial that some ask the
> “why” questions too. Ultimately, the trajectory of the Python story—and its true
> conclusion—is
>  at least in part up to you.
> Toward that end, this chapter begins by calling out what may be one of Python’s
> biggest downsides: its rate of change. This topic is unavoidably subjective, and
> you should weigh its coverage here on whatever scale you bring to the table.

### 中文翻译

> **《美好的终结》**
>
> 欢迎来到本书的结尾！既然你已经读到了这里，本章会先用几段话收束对 Python 演化的讨论，把你交还给软件开发领域，最后再用一点轻松的内容收尾。
>
> 你现在已经亲自接触过完整的 Python 语言，其中包括一些看起来似乎不太符合“应当让非专业人士也能使用的脚本语言”定位的高级特性。许多用户可以理解地把这些内容当作既成事实，但在开源项目中，必须有人继续追问“为什么”。最终，Python 故事的走向，以及它真正的结局，至少有一部分取决于你。
>
> 因此，本章先指出 Python 可能最大的缺点之一：变化速度太快。这个话题不可避免地带有主观性，你应当用自己的经验和标准来衡量下面的讨论。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：这一节把“会写 Python”提升为“能判断 Python 变化是否值得”。语言特性不是自动带来价值的清单；它们必须放在可读性、兼容性和维护成本中评估。
- **底层实现**：Python 程序通常经过解析、编译为字节码，再由实现（CPython、PyPy 等）执行；语言版本升级可能同时改变语法、标准库、字节码和实现行为。因此，“解释型语言”并不意味着版本变化没有运行时影响。
- **设计原因**：作者用收尾章节追问“为什么”，是因为开源语言会在长期演化中积累历史兼容和社区决策。理解设计背景，比机械记住某个新语法更能帮助工程师做版本选择。
- **实际问题**：维护项目时应记录支持的 Python 版本、锁定依赖、运行完整测试，并在升级前检查弃用警告和发布说明。这样可以把语言变化从突发事故变成有计划的工程工作。
- **初学者误区**：不要把“最新”误认为“最好”，也不要把“旧代码能运行”误认为“永远不需要升级”。先掌握稳定的核心语法，再按项目收益引入新特性。

---

## The Python Tsunami（Python 海啸）

### 英文原文

> The Python Tsunami
> Twelve years ago, this book warned that Python was growing too convoluted and
> bloated—and then Python grew a lot more convoluted and bloated. Clearly, this
> message has not reached those behind the convoluting and bloating.
> Even so, this stuff still matters. To parrot the Preface, the last dozen years have
> hosted the rise of f-string literals, named-assignment expressions, 
> match
> statements, type hinting, async coroutines, dictionary union, star-unpacking
> proliferation, underscore digit separators, module attribute hooks, exception
> groups, dictionary-key insertion order, positional-only function arguments, hash-
> based bytecode files, the 
> sys.executable
>  snub, and other superfluous
> additions, opinionated deprecations, and tangled mutations we’ve met along the
> way.
> Moreover, this 
> tsunami
>  of mods simply added to the flood of complexity and
> redundancy that came before it—including the oddly artificial MRO, the
> stunningly implicit 
> super
> , and the horrifically convoluted inheritance algorithm
> 
> of the preceding chapter, which elevates metaclasses and descriptors to
> prerequisites. The sum of these is an esoteric morass, which obfuscates the
> fundamental meaning of names in Python.
> Most of what should be said about these changes already has been said in this
> book, but their combined weight qualifies as 
> problematic for a tool that promotes
> itself as simpler than others. To illustrate, 
> Table 41-1
>  updates the prior edition’s
> accounting of Python’s largely unchecked growth so far—a partial but
> representative tally.

> If you care about Python, you should take a moment to browse this table. It
> reflects a virtual 
> explosion
>  of bifurcation, redundancy, and toolbox size—and 
> 81
> concepts that can all be required reading for both newcomers learning the
> language and experts reusing code written by others. Most of its categories
> began with just one original member in Python; many were added in part to
> imitate other languages; and none can be simplified today by pretending that the
> latest Python is the only Python that matters. Python 3.X now owns the flux in
> full.
> F-strings
>  are a prime example in this category. This book’s prior edition
> lamented the three redundant string-formatting tools of its time, but this set was
> subsequently expanded to a colossal four. While f-strings may be deemed a
> refinement by some, in truth they are a minor variation on a theme that adds yet
> another topic to the heap. More fundamentally, millions of programmers have
> written millions of programs using longer-lived options; while new code has the
> luxury of using new tools, pretending that the past didn’t happen constitutes a
> break with reality.
> Even extensions perhaps more unique often come loaded with surplus
> complexity. The 
> match
>  statement, for example, couldn’t simply provide a
> potentially useful multiple-choice option. It had to bolt on the conceptually
> tortuous and syntactically ad hoc structural pattern matching, which seems an
> answer to a question that nobody asked.
> Nor are additions the only user-unfriendly theme in Python. Its subjective
> changes
>  and 
> deprecations
>  are now so common that they must be expected as an
> implicit cost of using the language. To be clear, your Python code will almost
> certainly break eventually when you upgrade to a new Python release—and only
> because a Python core developer’s whim was made mandatory for everyone else.

### 中文翻译

> 十二年前，本书曾警告 Python 正变得过于纠缠、过于臃肿；然而之后 Python 又变得更加纠缠、更加臃肿。显然，这个消息并没有传到那些制造纠缠和臃肿的人那里。
>
> 即便如此，这些事情仍然重要。借用前言中的说法，过去十多年里出现了 f-string 字面量、命名赋值表达式、`match` 语句、类型提示、异步协程、字典并集、不断扩张的星号解包、下划线数字分隔符、模块属性钩子、异常组、字典键插入顺序、仅位置参数、基于哈希的字节码文件、对 `sys.executable` 的轻视，以及一路上遇到的其他多余添加、武断弃用和纠缠的变动。
>
> 更不用说，这场修改的“海啸”只是叠加到了更早以前已经存在的复杂性和冗余之上，其中包括人为感很强的 MRO、含义惊人地隐式的 `super`，以及上一章介绍的、极其纠缠的继承算法；后者甚至把元类和描述器提升成了先决知识。它们合在一起形成了一个深奥的泥潭，模糊了 Python 中名称的基本含义。
>
> 关于这些变化，大多数该说的话本书都已经说过了；但它们合在一起的重量，对于一个自称比其他工具更简单的工具而言，确实称得上是个问题。表 41-1 更新了上一版对 Python 至今几乎不受约束的增长所做的统计。这不是完整清单，但具有代表性。
>
> **表 41-1　Python 中冗余与工具爆炸的抽样统计**
>
> | 类别 | 成员 |
> |---|---|
> | 3 种主要范式 | 过程式、函数式、面向对象 |
> | 4 种字符串格式化工具 | `%` 表达式、`str.format`、`string.Template`、f-string |
> | 4 种属性访问工具 | property、descriptor、`__getattr__`、`__getattribute__` |
> | 2 种终结语句 | `try/finally`、`with` 加上下文管理器 |
> | 4 类推导式 | 列表、集合、字典、生成器 |
> | 3 种增强类的方式 | 手工重新绑定、`@` 装饰器、元类 |
> | 4 类方法 | 实例方法、静态方法、类方法、元类方法 |
> | 2 套属性存储系统 | `__dict__`、`__slots__` |
> | 4 种导入形式 | 模块、包、包相对导入、命名空间包 |
> | 2 种父类引用工具 | 显式类名、`super` 加 MRO |
> | 6 种赋值形式 | 基本、序列、多目标、`+=` 增强、`*` 解包、`:=` 命名赋值 |
> | 3 类函数 | 普通函数、`yield` 生成器、`async` 协程 |
> | 6 种函数参数形式 | 基本、`name=X`、`*X`、`**X`、仅关键字、仅位置 |
> | 2 种类行为来源 | 父类、元类 |
> | 3 种多选工具 | `if/elif`、字典索引、`match` |
> | 4 种保存状态的方式 | 类、闭包、函数属性、可变对象 |
> | 2 种字节码存储方案 | 时间戳、哈希键 |
> | 3 种名称字符串导入器 | `exec`、`__import__`、`importlib` |
> | 2 类装饰器 | 函数装饰器、类装饰器 |
> | 4 种合并字典的方式 | `for` 循环、`update` 方法、`**D` 解包、`|` 并集运算符 |
> | 2 种异常处理模型 | 单个 `except`、异常组 `except*` |
> | 4 种把语句伪装成表达式的形式 | `if/else`、推导式、`lambda`、`:=` 赋值表达式 |
> | 8 种带星号的收集/解包位置 | 赋值、函数定义、函数调用、列表/元组/字典/集合字面量、`match` |
>
> 如果你关心 Python，不妨花一点时间浏览这张表。它反映出分叉、冗余和工具箱规模的虚拟“爆炸”：多达 81 个概念可能同时成为学习语言的新手和复用他人代码的专家的必读内容。大多数类别在 Python 中最初只有一个成员；许多成员部分是为了模仿其他语言而加入的；如今也不能假装“只有最新版 Python 才重要”来简化问题。Python 3.x 已经完整地接管了这种流变。
>
> f-string 是其中一个典型例子。本书上一版曾批评当时已有的三种冗余字符串格式化工具，但后来又扩展成了庞大的四种。有人可以把 f-string 视为改进，但它本质上只是同一主题的轻微变体，又向知识堆里增加了一个主题。更根本的是，数百万程序员已经用寿命更长的旧选项写出了数百万个程序；新代码可以享受新工具，但假装过去从未发生过，就是脱离现实。
>
> 即使是更独特的扩展，也经常携带多余的复杂性。例如，`match` 语句本可以只提供一个有用的多路选择工具，却还要附加概念上曲折、语法上临时拼接的结构化模式匹配，看起来像是在回答一个没人提出的问题。
>
> 添加新特性也不是 Python 唯一不友好的主题。它的主观变化和弃用现在如此频繁，以至于使用这门语言就必须默认承担这种隐性成本。明确地说，当你升级到新的 Python 发行版时，代码几乎肯定最终会因为某个 Python 核心开发者的偏好被强制化而出现问题。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：作者批评的是“工具箱膨胀”和语义分叉，而不只是某一个语法。一个任务有多种等价写法，会增加阅读者必须掌握的上下文数量；表中的 81 个概念说明兼容性本身已经成为 Python 能力的一部分。
- **底层实现**：版本变化可能影响解析器、AST、编译出的字节码、导入系统和运行时对象协议。例如，`match` 会引入新的语法与模式匹配语义，`except*` 会引入异常组处理路径；旧解释器无法解析新语法，不能靠运行时兼容来弥补。
- **设计原因**：新特性通常试图解决真实需求或吸收其他语言的实践，但“能加入”不等于“应该加入”。保留旧工具是兼容性承诺，新增工具则会扩大文档、培训、测试和维护的长期面积。
- **实际问题**：工程团队应在公共代码风格中选定一种格式化、导入、异常处理和数据合并方式，避免同一项目混用四种写法；升级时用 CI 矩阵验证目标版本，并把 `DeprecationWarning` 当成待办事项。
- **初学者误区**：不要为了显得现代而把所有新语法塞进代码，也不要因为作者批评某特性就拒绝理解它。正确做法是知道特性的语义、适用版本和迁移成本，再根据项目的支持范围选择。

## The Python Sandbox（Python 沙盒）

### 英文原文
> The Python Sandbox
> All of this stems from the fact that Python 
> is, and probably always has been, a
> constantly morphing 
> sandbox
>  of ideas, which prioritizes its developers’ egos
> over its users’ needs. Playing in a sandbox can be fun, of course, but it’s lousy
> for the millions of people downstream from its churn. These people are simply
> trying to write software that’s reliable and durable. Like all engineering
> 
> endeavors, that works best with a 
> stable base
> , not constantly shifting sand.
> As a pathological example, Python’s sandbox model seems to have hit its zenith
> in 
> type hinting
> —the optional, unused, out-of-place, and embarrassingly
> academic subdomain we glanced in 
> Chapter 6
>  but largely omitted here by
> design. This is unchecked convolution on parade, and leaves Python users to
> puzzle over the paradox of pointless type declarations in a dynamically typed
> language. Sadly, it’s also likely to appeal to control freaks.
> As we’ve also seen regularly in this book, because the sandbox is oriented
> toward experts, it inevitably produces tools that assume that you have to already
> be an expert to use them. Classes and OOP, for example, are required skills for
> even simple exception handling. Python is not just for its developers, but the
> forward knowledge
>  assumptions of many of its additions embed this message
> and raise the bar for newcomers unnecessarily and unkindly.
> To be fair, it’s not just Python: the entire software field is permeated by a 
> culture
> of change
>  in which churn is an expected constant, and prowess often consists of
> flaunting the latest and greatest tools even when they are unwarranted. This
> doesn’t prove intelligence (and often demos its absence), but annual and
> mandatory mods are now a norm. Whether one breaks your PC, smartphone, or
> Python code, it’s difficult not to see this as divisive and rude.

### 中文翻译

> 这一切源于一个事实：Python 是，而且很可能一直都是，一个不断变形的思想“沙盒”，它把开发者的自我满足置于用户需求之上。在沙盒里玩当然可能很有趣，但对于下游数百万只想写出可靠、持久软件的人来说，这种不断变化的状态非常糟糕。像所有工程工作一样，软件在稳定的基础上运行得最好，而不是建在不断移动的沙地上。
>
> 一个极端例子是，Python 的沙盒模式似乎在类型提示（type hinting）上达到了顶峰。类型提示是一个可选、通常不改变运行时、位置尴尬而且带有学术色彩的子领域；我们在第 6 章略有提及，但本书有意基本省略了它。这是未经约束的复杂化示范，让 Python 用户困惑于一个悖论：动态类型语言中，为什么要声明一些没有运行时用途的类型？它还很可能吸引喜欢绝对控制的人。
>
> 正如本书经常展示的那样，因为这个沙盒面向专家，它不可避免地制造出一些假设使用者已经是专家的工具。比如，即使是简单的异常处理，也被要求掌握类和 OOP。Python 不只是为它的开发者服务；许多新增内容对使用者知识的预设也在传递同一个信息，并且不必要、不友好地抬高了新手的门槛。
>
> 公平地说，这不只是 Python 的问题：整个软件行业都浸泡在一种“变化文化”里，持续变化被视为常态，能力常常被表现为炫耀最新、最强的工具，即使那些工具并不必要。这不代表聪明，很多时候反而暴露了缺乏判断；但每年一次、强制性的修改如今已成为规范。无论它破坏的是电脑、手机还是 Python 代码，都很难不觉得这种做法具有分裂性，也不够尊重用户。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：沙盒比喻强调“语言可以实验”与“用户需要稳定”之间的张力。开发者试验新工具的成本，往往由下游应用、培训材料和长期维护者承担。
- **底层实现**：类型注解在常规 Python 执行中主要保存在函数或类的 `__annotations__` 中，由解释器和第三方工具按约定读取；静态检查器、运行时校验器和框架会赋予它不同语义。因此，类型提示不会自动把动态类型 Python 变成静态类型语言。
- **设计原因**：专家工具可以扩展表达能力，但如果新特性依赖元类、描述器或复杂的导入规则，就会把前置知识链条继续拉长。语言设计需要同时考虑专家的能力上限和新手的入门路径。
- **实际问题**：生产项目应区分“运行时必需”的约束和“工具链可选”的检查；升级前要在隔离环境中验证编辑器、类型检查器、测试框架和部署镜像，而不是只验证解释器能否启动。
- **初学者误区**：类型提示是工具，不是运行时真相；也不要因一个库使用高级类型系统，就把整个项目的公共 API 设计成难以阅读的类型谜题。先建立清晰的运行时行为，再决定是否增加静态约束。

## The Python Upside（Python 的优势）

### 英文原文
> The Python Upside
> All that being said, it’s also difficult to deny that Python, despite its warts, is still
> more productive and pleasant to use than other programming languages. If
> you’ve coded other languages, you know that many come laden with extraneous
> syntax and rules, which seem to reflect an assumption that programmers cannot
> be trusted to do their jobs. By sharp contrast, Python’s dynamic typing and
> innate flexibility make it more ally than obstacle.
> Python’s rise in 
> popularity
>  seems to attest to this value proposition, though it’s
> impetus may be 
> more practical than academic. Today’s larger Python world may
> naturally be less concerned with the language’s original and perhaps idealistic
> goals than with solving concrete problems. In the real world that hosts Python
> popularity, arcane language topics usually take a back seat to libraries, platforms,
> and schedules—calls that Python has always answered in full.
> 
> Moreover, some change and complexity is 
> warranted
>  in software. Programming
> is a substantially challenging task (despite what you may have heard), and
> computer science is a field still young enough to be excused for some youthful
> thrashing. For Python specifically, though, complexity and thrashing should be
> modulated by broad appeal.
> In the end, the Python language remains a remarkably expressive tool that still
> fits both programming tasks and your brain as well as it ever did. Especially if
> you stick to its tried-and-true parts that propelled Python to the top of the
> language charts, you’ll likely find it an enabling technology that makes coding
> as much fun as chore.
> Prudent engineers, though, would do well to exercise caution when upgrading to
> the leading edge, and give a pass to the sandbox’s annual outflow except when
> clearly beneficial. Given that this is now an industry-wide requirement, it’s
> hardly cause to dismiss an otherwise useful tool. Bad practice, however, does not
> justify bad practice.

### 中文翻译

> 话虽如此，也很难否认：尽管 Python 有各种缺点，它仍然比其他编程语言更高效、更令人愉快。如果你写过其他语言，就知道其中许多语言带着额外的语法和规则，仿佛默认程序员不值得信任。相比之下，Python 的动态类型和天生的灵活性更像盟友，而不是障碍。
>
> Python 受欢迎程度的上升似乎证明了这一价值主张，不过它的推动力可能更多来自实践而非学术。如今更庞大的 Python 世界自然可能不太关心语言最初、也许更理想主义的目标，而更关心解决具体问题。在 Python 流行的现实世界里，晦涩的语言议题通常让位于库、平台和进度安排，而 Python 一直都能很好地回应这些需求。
>
> 此外，软件中的某些变化和复杂性是合理的。编程本来就是一项相当有挑战的工作，计算机科学也还年轻到可以被原谅一些成长时期的摇摆。但对于 Python，复杂性和摇摆仍应受到“面向广泛用户”的目标调节。
>
> 归根结底，Python 仍是一种极具表达力的工具，仍然像过去一样同时贴合编程任务和人的思维。尤其是当你坚持使用那些可靠、成熟、曾把 Python 推到语言排行榜前列的部分时，你很可能会发现它是一种让编程更像乐趣而不是苦差事的赋能技术。
>
> 但谨慎的工程师在升级到最前沿版本时应保持警惕，除非新变化明确有益，否则可以跳过沙盒每年的产出。既然这已经成为整个行业的要求，它还不足以成为放弃这个有用工具的理由；不过，坏的实践不会因为行业普遍存在就变成好的实践。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：Python 的优势是开发者生产力和表达力，而不是“所有场景下运行最快”。本节的结论是承认复杂性成本，同时继续使用 Python 最成熟、最能减少样板代码的部分。
- **底层实现**：动态类型让名称在运行时绑定对象，减少了编译期声明，但也把一部分错误推迟到测试或执行路径；跨平台能力则依赖解释器、标准库和操作系统适配层，而不是源代码天然没有边界。
- **设计原因**：语言复杂度并非绝对坏事，关键是收益是否覆盖学习、兼容和维护成本。Python 的核心价值来自少量一致的机制，因此工程实践应优先保留这些机制，而不是追逐每个新增特性。
- **实际问题**：版本选择应结合依赖支持、部署平台、性能需求和团队熟悉度。新项目可以评估当前稳定版，长期维护项目则应先在兼容矩阵和预发布环境中验证，再安排升级窗口。
- **初学者误区**：不要把“Python 易学”理解为“Python 只有简单功能”，也不要把“Python 灵活”理解为可以随意牺牲可读性。灵活性越高，越需要项目约定和自动化测试来约束结果。

## Closing Thoughts（收束思考）

### 英文原文
> Closing Thoughts
> So there you have it: some observations from the trenches, born of three decades
> using, teaching, and advocating Python, and grounded in a desire to improve the
> Python story.
> None of these concerns are entirely new. Indeed, the growth of this very book
> over the years seems a testament to that of Python itself—if not an ironic eulogy
> to a mission statement that once stressed simplification of programming, and
> accessibility to both experts and nonprofessionals alike. Judging by language
> heft alone, that dream seems to have been either neglected over time or
> abandoned entirely.
> But we can do better. A well-established tool like Python can afford to focus
> more on its users’ needs than its changers’ hubris. Per the old adage, we simply
> have to stop fixing what isn’t broken. If we can, it will go far toward addressing
> the concerns of those vetting the language for projects that cannot afford to
> budget for shifting sands.
> More importantly, in an open source project like Python the answers to such
> 
> questions must be formed anew by each wave of newcomers. Hopefully, the
> wave you ride in will have as much common sense as fun while plotting
> Python’s future.

### 中文翻译

> 以上就是全部内容：这是一些来自一线的观察，源自三十年使用、教授和倡导 Python 的经历，也源于改进 Python 故事的愿望。
>
> 这些担忧并不完全是新问题。事实上，这本书多年来的增长似乎正是 Python 自身增长的见证；如果不是对那个曾经强调简化编程、让专家和非专业人士都能接触编程的使命宣言所作的讽刺性悼词的话。单从语言的重量来看，那个梦想似乎随着时间被忽视了，甚至被放弃了。
>
> 但我们可以做得更好。像 Python 这样已经建立起来的工具，完全可以把用户需求置于变更者的自负之上。按照那句老话，我们只需要停止修理没有坏掉的东西。如果做到这一点，就能大幅回应那些正在为无法承受持续变化成本的项目评估语言的人所提出的担忧。
>
> 更重要的是，在 Python 这样的开源项目中，这些问题的答案必须由一批又一批的新来者重新形成。希望你所乘的那一波浪潮，在规划 Python 未来时既有乐趣，也有足够的常识。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：收束部分把语言演化重新放回开源治理和用户责任中。Python 的未来不是核心开发者单方面决定的，也取决于使用者是否提出稳定性、可读性和长期维护的要求。
- **底层实现**：一个语言生态的兼容性不仅由解释器决定，还由标准库、第三方包、构建工具、操作系统和部署镜像共同决定；任何一层的升级都可能改变最终程序的行为。
- **设计原因**：作者强调“不要修理没坏的东西”，并非反对所有进步，而是要求变化有明确收益、迁移路径和回滚方案。成熟系统应把稳定当作一种特性，而不是缺乏创新。
- **实际问题**：团队可以用支持策略、版本锁定、变更记录、回归测试和灰度发布把个人偏好转化为可审计决策。评估新语法时同时记录最低解释器版本和替代写法，能减少未来迁移压力。
- **初学者误区**：不要把书中作者的批评当成 Python 的官方结论；这是作者基于长期经验的观点。学习者应能区分原文立场、语言事实和自己的工程取舍。

## Where to Go from Here（接下来去哪里）

### 英文原文
> Where to Go from Here
> And that’s a wrap, folks. You’ve officially reached the end of this book. Now
> that you know Python inside and out, your next step, should you choose to take
> it, is to explore the libraries, techniques, and tools available in the application
> domains in which you will work.
> Because Python is so widely used, you’ll find ample resources for using it in
> almost any domain you can think of—from GUIs, the web, and apps, to numeric
> programming, databases, and system administration. See 
> Chapter 1
>  and your
> favorite web browser for pointers to popular tools and topics.
> This is where Python starts to become truly fun, but this is also where this book’s
> story ends, and those of other resources begin. Good luck with your journey.
> And as always: code well!

### 中文翻译

> 好了，各位，这就是收尾。你已经正式读完本书。现在你已经从里到外了解了 Python，下一步（如果你愿意）就是探索你将工作的应用领域中可用的库、技术和工具。
>
> Python 被广泛使用，因此几乎任何你能想到的领域都有足够的资源：GUI、Web、应用程序、数值编程、数据库和系统管理等。可以回看第 1 章，也可以使用你喜欢的浏览器查找热门工具和主题。
>
> Python 真正开始变得有趣的地方就在这里；但这也是本书故事结束、其他资源故事开始的地方。祝你的旅程顺利。也一如既往：写出好的代码！

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：基础语言学习的终点是应用领域的起点。Python 的实际价值通常来自标准库和第三方生态，而不是单独掌握语法。
- **底层实现**：GUI、Web、数值计算和系统工具会分别接触事件循环、网络协议、原生扩展、进程与文件系统等运行时边界；选择库时必须理解这些边界，而不能只看 API 表面。
- **设计原因**：Python 通过统一的对象、模块和调用模型连接不同领域，使学习成本可以跨库迁移；这种通用性也是其生态持续扩大的基础。
- **实际问题**：进入新领域时，应先确认 Python 版本、平台、依赖许可证、性能约束和部署方式，再选择库并建立最小可运行样例。先做小实验，比直接搭建完整系统更容易发现兼容问题。
- **初学者误区**：读完语言书不等于掌握某个领域。不要把库的示例代码当作生产架构，也不要忽视错误处理、日志、测试、升级和安全边界。

### 中文翻译

> 最后还有一件事：本书这一部分不再安排练习，例 41-1 提供了一个可以自行研究和运行的加餐脚本。书本不能直接给读者发放完成证书（如果可以，证书也会失去价值），但可以提供一个虽然有点俗气、确实能生成证书的 Python 脚本。这个脚本会同时创建纯文本和 HTML 格式的简单读书完成证书，并在支持的环境中自动用浏览器或其他查看器打开它们。
>
> 脚本的文件名是 `You-made-it.py`。开头的文档字符串说明它会把证书打印到控制台，同时保存成自动打开的文本和 HTML 文件；在手机上可能需要手动打开文件。程序导入 `time`、`sys`、`html` 和 `os`，设置分隔线宽度、是否启动浏览器、输出文件名前缀，并用当前时间和用户输入构造证书文本。
>
> `texttext` 是一个 f-string 模板：它把分隔线、日期、读者姓名和书名嵌入证书正文，并给读者接下来学习网站、桌面 GUI、科学模型、手机应用以及未来计算方向的“通行权”。随后程序逐字打印大写的祝贺语，刷新标准输出并短暂休眠，让启动过程具有动画效果。
>
> 脚本先把正文写入 `Certificate.txt`。接着，它把分隔线替换成 HTML 容器，把空行转换成段落，把制表符开头的行转换成斜体缩进，并将结果嵌入包含样式、图标链接和页脚的 HTML 文档，最后写入 `Certificate.html`。程序还会把文本文件内容打印到控制台，并尝试用 `webbrowser` 打开两个文件；自动打开失败时会提示用户手动处理。
>
> 你可以在控制台运行 `python3 You-made-it.py`。在电脑上脚本通常可以完整工作，某些系统还可以通过 `os.startfile`、`open` 或 `xdg-open` 打开文件；在手机上则很可能需要用文件管理器手动打开输出。例 41-1 的 HTML 结果见图 41-1。作者还建议读者把它当作回顾本书思想的小练习，并忽略不属于本书范围的 HTML 细节。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：证书脚本把本章的“从语言走向工程”落到一个完整的小程序上：读取输入、格式化数据、写入文件、生成 HTML、调用平台能力和处理异常。
- **底层实现**：f-string 在运行时求值并生成普通 `str`；`open(..., encoding='utf8')` 创建文本 I/O 对象，写入时由编码器把 Unicode 文本转换为字节；`webbrowser` 最终依赖操作系统的默认应用关联。`with open(...)` 会更稳妥地保证关闭文件，但原文示例使用显式 `close()`，应理解两者的工程差异。
- **设计原因**：脚本同时生成纯文本和 HTML，是“数据内容”和“表现形式”分离的简单示范；先建立 `texttext`，再做替换，可以避免重复维护两份证书内容。
- **实际问题**：生产代码应使用 `pathlib.Path` 管理路径，避免固定当前工作目录；应在写文件和启动外部查看器时处理权限、编码、文件覆盖和平台差异，并考虑不可信姓名输入对 HTML 的转义。
- **初学者误区**：`html.escape` 只处理被显式调用的字符串；把原始用户输入直接拼到 HTML 中可能造成注入。另一个误区是把 `time.sleep()` 的展示效果当作业务逻辑，它会阻塞当前线程，只适合这个演示脚本。

### 代码分析

```python
"""
生成一个简单的课程完成证书：打印到控制台，并保存到
自动打开的文本和 HTML 文件中。
从控制台运行；如有需要可以打印保存的输出文件。
适用于所有 PC，但在手机上可能需要手动打开文件。
"""
import time, sys, html, os

maxline = 60                 # 文本分隔线长度
browser = True               # 是否在浏览器中显示
saveto = 'Certificate'       # 输出文件名前缀
# 模板值
SEPT = '*' * maxline
DATE = time.strftime('%A, %b %d, %Y, %I:%M %p')
NAME = input('Please enter your name: ').strip() or 'An unknown reader'
BOOK = 'Learning Python, 6th Edition'
SITE = 'https://learning-python.com'  # 图标、图片和链接使用

# f-string 模板可以读取代码中预先设置的引用
texttext = f"""
{SEPT}

 Official Certificate 

Date: {DATE}
This certifies that:
\t{NAME}
Has survived the massive tome:
\t{BOOK}
And is now entitled to all privileges thereof, including
the right to proceed on to learning how to develop websites,
desktop GUIs, scientific models, smartphone apps, and 
anything else that the future of computing may hold. 
--Your humble instructor
(Note: void where obtained by skipping ahead.)
{SEPT}
"""

# 交互和动画
for c in 'Congratulations!'.upper() + '\n' * 3:
    print(c, end=' ')
    sys.stdout.flush()       # 某些 shell 会等待换行
    time.sleep(0.25)         # 让消息逐字显示
print()
time.sleep(3)

# 创建文本文件
textto = saveto + '.txt'
fileto = open(textto, 'w', encoding='utf8')
print(texttext, file=fileto)
fileto.close()

# 创建 HTML：把文本标记替换为标签
htmltext = texttext.replace(SEPT, '<div class=cert>', 1)
htmltext = htmltext.replace(SEPT, '</div>')
htmltext = htmltext.replace('\n\n', '<h1 align=center>\n\n&nbsp;', 1)
htmltext = htmltext.replace(' \n\n', '&nbsp;\n\n</h1>')

# 逐行调整
linemods = []
for line in htmltext.split('\n'):
    if line == '':
        line = '<p>'
    elif line[:1] == '\t':
        line = f"<i>{'&nbsp;' * 4}{html.escape(line[1:])}</i>"
    linemods.append(line)
htmltext = '\n'.join(linemods)

# HTML 外壳；f-string 中的花括号需要转义
preamble = f'''<!doctype html>
<html>
<head>
<meta http-equiv="Content-Type" content="text/html; charset=UTF-8">
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1.0">
<link rel="icon" type="image/x-icon" href="{SITE}/favicon.ico">
<style>
body {{font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;}}
.cert {{background-color: cornsilk; padding: 16px; border: medium solid black;}}
</style>
<title>LP6E Completion Certificate</title>
</head>
'''
image, page = 'lp6e-large.jpg', 'about-lp.html'
footer = f'''
<table><tr>
<td><a href="{SITE}/{page}"><img src="{SITE}/{image}" hspace=10 height=50></a>
<td><a href="{SITE}/{page}" align=center><i>Book support site</i></a>
</tr></table>
'''

# 合并 HTML 内容
htmltext = f'{preamble}<body bgcolor="#eee">{htmltext}{footer}</body></html>'
htmlto = saveto + '.html'
fileto = open(htmlto, 'w', encoding='utf8')
print(htmltext, file=fileto)
fileto.close()

# 在控制台显示文本结果
print(f'[File: {textto}]', end='')
print('\n' * 2, open(textto, encoding='utf8').read())

# 尝试打开文档；失败时静默转为手动打开
if browser:
    try:
        import webbrowser
        for doc in (textto, htmlto):
            webbrowser.open('file://' + os.path.abspath(doc))
    except Exception:
        print('Unable to auto-open docs: open manually.')
input('[Press Enter to close]')
```

- 解释器先导入模块并在全局作用域创建配置值；`'*' * maxline` 产生长度固定的字符串，f-string 在构造 `texttext` 时读取当前变量值。
- `input()` 返回字符串，`strip()` 去掉首尾空白，`or` 在输入为空时选择默认名称。写文件时指定 `encoding='utf8'`，使文本编码不依赖平台默认值。
- 原文使用字符串替换把文本模板转换成 HTML，并通过 `html.escape` 处理制表符行。脚本属于教学示例，实际项目应优先使用模板库或更严格的 HTML 转义策略。
- `webbrowser.open()` 是平台相关的外部副作用，异常捕获只保证脚本不会因自动打开失败而直接终止，并不保证所有系统都能找到浏览器。
> 
> Part IX. 
> Appendixes

## Appendix A. Platform Usage Tips（平台使用提示）

### 英文原文
> 
> Appendix A. 
> Platform Usage Tips
> One of Python’s main strengths is its 
> portability
> : most of the code you’ll write in
> your Python programs will work the same on all computing platforms. This has
> limits, of course; programs that use Python’s portable libraries weather device
> hops better than others, and platform idiosyncrasies and restrictions can
> sometimes pose interoperability hurdles that require special handling. But by and
> large, the Python language is cross-platform by design.
> Python’s portability means you can run its code on just about every computing
> device on the planet—from smartphones and tablets to PCs and supercomputers
> —and each of these systems has unique setup and usage details. While this
> appendix cannot be an exhaustive user guide for every one of those devices, it
> provides just enough info to help you prepare to run this book’s code examples
> on your popular platform—or platforms—of choice, including Windows,
> macOS, Linux, Android, and iOS.
> Before we get started, here are two quick content notes up front. First, because
> you’re going to have enough on your plate just learning Python itself, the focus
> throughout this appendix is on 
> keeping it simple
> . There are many ways to run
> code, and you may find advanced options useful once you graduate from Python
> novice to master. Especially when starting out, though, this book recommends
> walking the easiest path.
> Second, a usage appendix like this is unavoidably doomed to grow out of date
> soon, given the rapid and constant change in the computing world (even the
> name of macOS, after all, has changed repeatedly on this book’s watch!). Hence,
> please consider this a 
> snapshot
>  of the current state and practice, and plan to
> consult the latest resources 
> if
>  when this story changes. For the present, let’s jump
> right into today’s usage options—while they last.

### 中文翻译

> Python 的主要优势之一是可移植性（portability）：你在 Python 程序中编写的大多数代码，在所有计算平台上都能以相同方式工作。当然这有边界；使用 Python 可移植库的程序比其他程序更能经受设备迁移，但平台特性和限制有时会造成互操作障碍，需要特殊处理。不过总体而言，Python 语言从设计上就是跨平台的。
>
> Python 的可移植性意味着它几乎可以运行在地球上各种计算设备中，从手机和平板到个人电脑和超级计算机；而每种系统都有独特的安装和使用细节。本附录不可能为每种设备提供完整用户指南，但会提供足够的信息，帮助你在 Windows、macOS、Linux、Android 和 iOS 等常见平台上运行本书示例。
>
> 本附录始终把“保持简单”作为重点，因为学习 Python 本身已经有足够多的内容。运行代码有许多方法，等你从新手成长为熟练者后，可能会需要高级选项；但刚开始时，本书建议走最容易的路径。
>
> 还要注意，计算世界变化很快，这样的使用附录很快就会过时，甚至 macOS 的名称在本书编写期间也反复改变。因此，应把本附录视为当前状态和实践的一个快照；情况变化时请查阅最新资源。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：可移植性是“语言和标准库尽量统一”，不是“所有操作系统行为都相同”。路径、权限、终端、GUI、文件关联和应用商店仍然是平台边界。
- **底层实现**：Python 解释器把源代码语义映射到各平台的文件系统、套接字、进程和窗口系统；标准库提供抽象层，但无法消除底层能力差异。
- **设计原因**：附录优先推荐简单、稳定的安装和启动方式，是为了减少学习者同时处理解释器、IDE、包管理器和操作系统配置的认知负担。
- **实际问题**：安装时要记录解释器路径和版本，创建隔离环境并用 `python -m pip` 绑定正确解释器；跨平台项目应使用 `pathlib`、环境变量和 CI 矩阵验证，而不是假设开发机行为就是部署机行为。
- **初学者误区**：`python`、`python3`、`py`、IDE 启动按钮可能指向不同解释器；看到“已安装 Python”不等于命令行、编辑器和项目虚拟环境使用的是同一个版本。

>  trick won’t help if the program commits an

> .py
>  opens a console for standard

> Windows command-line interfaces use the

>  (a.k.a. 
> Path

> variable to locate named programs like

> , used on Windows to specify the
> default Unicode encoding of files and redirected streams (this convoluted
> story has changed in 3.X often and will again; see 
> Chapter 37
> ).
> Other Windows options
> Most Python code works the same on Windows as on other platforms,
> especially if it uses Python’s portable system tools in modules like 
> os
> . In
> cases where Windows-specific tools are required, though, the 
> pywin32
>  third-
> party extension allows your Python programs to access many Windows APIs
> 
> directly.
> For more about using Python on Windows, try 
> python.org
> ’s 
> HOWTO
>  as well as
> the 
> copious resources on the web (with the usual caution about vetting their
> copious sources). Here, let’s move on to the next PC platform on our tour.

### 中文翻译

> Windows 是个人电脑市场份额的当前领先者，因此很可能是许多读者第一次接触 Python 的平台。Python 从早期开始就能在 Windows 上完整使用，并且尽力把 Windows 的专有差异隔离起来，让你的代码不必承担这些差异。比如，编写良好的 Unix Python 程序经常可以在 Windows 上不加修改地运行。
>
> 如今在 Windows 上使用 Python 至少有三种方式：直接在 Windows 中运行、使用 Windows Subsystem for Linux（WSL/WSL2），或者使用第三方类 Unix 环境 Cygwin。本书不展开 Cygwin，因为它的使用范围较小，真正需要它的读者通常已经知道如何使用。
>
> WSL（包括较新的 WSL2）可以在不进行双系统安装、不准备另一台设备的情况下把 Linux 带到 Windows 电脑上。它在 Windows 界面中运行标准 Linux 发行版，例如 Ubuntu，并避免传统虚拟机的一些取舍。WSL 提供编辑和运行 Python 的命令行，WSL2 还可以运行 Linux GUI 应用；由于 WSL 本质上是 Linux，具体安装和使用可参考后面的 Linux 部分以及 Microsoft 文档。不过它需要额外配置，当前也并非完全没有摩擦。对大多数读者来说，最简单的方式仍是在 Windows 中原生安装 Python。
>
> Python 不随 Windows 预装，但安装和使用都很容易。可以从 `python.org` 下载 Windows 自安装程序，也可以从 Microsoft Store 安装；安装后可通过命令行、图形化 IDE（例如 IDLE）或文件资源管理器中的 `.py` 文件启动。
>
> 本书推荐从 `python.org` 下载 Python 3.x 自安装程序，并同时安装 IDLE 和标准库。安装时通常可以接受默认值，但建议选择把 Python 加入 `PATH`，并在最后解除 Windows 的路径长度限制。Microsoft Store 版本也来自同一来源，但可能有访问限制；除非有明确需求，不要同时安装 Store 和非 Store 版本，以免命令关联混乱。
>
> 在 Windows 命令行中可以打开 Command Prompt 或 PowerShell，输入 `py` 启动交互式 REPL，输入 `py script.py` 运行脚本。Windows launcher 默认选择已安装的最新 Python，也可以用 `py -3` 或 `py -3.8` 指定版本。若安装时修改了 `PATH`，也可以使用 `python`；给命令追加 `> filename.txt` 可以重定向输出。
>
> 命令行方式还需要一个文本编辑器。Notepad 足够编写示例，IDLE 则同时提供 Python 友好的编辑器和运行环境。IDLE 的 `Run -> Run Module`（或 F5）会先保存文件，再在 Shell 窗口中执行；也可以通过 `py -m idlelib.idle` 启动它。
>
> Windows 还会为 `.py` 文件设置文件关联，所以可以在命令行直接输入文件名，也可以双击文件运行。双击启动的控制台可能在程序退出时关闭，导致输出消失；可以在脚本末尾使用 `input()` 暂停窗口，但如果程序在到达这一行前报错，错误信息仍可能随窗口关闭而消失。GUI 程序通常可使用 `.pyw` 文件避免打开控制台。
>
> 高级提示包括：`#!` 首行可以让 Windows launcher 选择版本；`PATH` 决定命令搜索位置，`PYTHONPATH` 影响模块搜索，`PYTHONUTF8` 和 `PYTHONIOENCODING` 影响编码行为；需要访问 Windows 专有 API 时可以考虑第三方 `pywin32`。详细信息应以 Python 官方 HOWTO 和当前系统文档为准。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：Windows 的重点不是“Python 不能跨平台”，而是启动器、路径、文件关联和终端退出规则不同。原生 Windows、WSL 和 Cygwin 是不同运行环境，不能把它们的解释器和包环境混为一谈。
- **底层实现**：`py` 是 Windows launcher，不是 Python 语言本身；它根据命令行参数和 `#!` 指示选择解释器。`PATH` 由 shell 用来查找可执行文件，`PYTHONPATH` 则参与 Python 的模块搜索，两者解决的是不同层次的问题。
- **设计原因**：Windows launcher 和 `.py` 文件关联降低了启动门槛；WSL 提供 Unix 工具链则服务于需要 Linux 用户空间的项目。它们是不同的工程取舍，不是互相替代的“正确答案”。
- **实际问题**：运行 `python -m pip`、`py -m pip` 或虚拟环境中的解释器来确认包安装到了正确环境；排查 `ModuleNotFoundError` 时先检查 `sys.executable`、工作目录和 `sys.path`，不要盲目重复安装。
- **初学者误区**：PowerShell、Command Prompt、WSL 终端中的 `python` 可能指向不同版本；双击脚本看不到输出也不代表程序没有运行。开发阶段优先用显式命令行和日志，完成后再配置文件关联。

### 代码分析

```text
py                 # 启动默认的最新 Python 3.x
py -3.8            # 请求 Python 3.8
py script.py       # 运行脚本
python -m pip      # 使用当前 python 对应的 pip
```

- 命令行先由 shell 解析参数，再由 launcher 或 `PATH` 找到解释器；解释器随后把脚本编译成字节码并执行。
- 重定向符 `>` 属于 shell 语法，不是 Python 运算符；它把标准输出交给文件，标准错误是否重定向取决于 shell 写法。
- `#!` 在 Windows 上由 launcher 解析，在 Unix 上通常由内核和 shell 处理。相同的文本在不同平台上可能由不同组件解释，这正是启动方式需要记录的原因。

### 中文翻译

> macOS 基于 Unix，非常适合 Python、开源软件和一般的软件开发。较新的 macOS 电脑已经不再预装可直接使用的 Python；旧系统中的 Python 可能是已经过时的 2.x，启动时还可能发出弃用警告，不能用来运行本书示例，因此需要单独安装。
>
> 推荐从 `python.org` 的 Downloads 页面获取 macOS 自安装程序。它会安装 Python、IDLE 和标准库，包括 `tkinter` GUI 工具包。当前安装包是 Universal 2 二进制文件，可以原生运行在 Apple Silicon（ARM）和 Intel（x86）Mac 上，也可以在 Rosetta 2 中运行。
>
> 如果在运行 `python.org` 安装程序前于 Terminal 输入 `python3`，macOS 可能会弹出 Xcode 命令行开发工具安装提示。大多数用户应忽略这个提示，直接使用独立的 `python.org` 安装，以免 Python 受工具包的版本和配置影响。
>
> 安装后可以从 Launchpad 或 Finder 的 Applications 启动工具，也可以在 Terminal 中使用 `python3`。输入 `python3` 启动交互会话，输入 `python3 script.py` 运行文件。macOS 的 REPL 使用 Control+D 退出；也可以在 shell 启动文件中给 `python3` 设置更短的别名。
>
> 用命令行编写代码时，可以使用 TextEdit（先调整为等宽字体），也可以使用 IDLE、vi、nano 等编辑器。Python Launcher 可以通过 Finder 单击或拖动运行代码文件，IDLE 提供编辑和运行 Python 的 GUI。IDLE 使用 Python 编写的 `tkinter` GUI，因此在支持的平台上界面和行为大体一致。
>
> macOS 的高级用法包括：shell 会把以 `#!` 开头的首行交给指定解释器；Finder 运行脚本时可以配置 Python Launcher；`PATH` 用来寻找 `python3`，`PYTHONPATH` 用来寻找导入模块，这些环境变量可以写在 `~/.bash_profile` 或 `~/.zprofile` 中。PyObjC 和 Homebrew 提供其他方案，但额外配置更适合熟练用户。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：macOS 的 Python 使用体验更接近 Unix，但“系统里有一个 Python”不等于它适合项目开发。解释器来源、架构（ARM/x86）和 shell 配置都会影响结果。
- **底层实现**：`python3` 是由 `PATH` 找到的可执行文件；`#!` 由 shell/内核启动链处理。Universal 2 安装包包含适配两种 CPU 架构的内容，Rosetta 2 则提供架构翻译。
- **设计原因**：独立安装 Python 可以把语言版本从 macOS 和 Xcode 的工具链中解耦，减少系统升级或开发工具升级对项目的意外影响。
- **实际问题**：在 Apple Silicon 上要确认原生与 Rosetta 环境下的解释器和原生扩展是否一致；在项目中使用虚拟环境并记录 `which python3`、`python3 -V` 和 `sys.executable`。
- **初学者误区**：不要删除或替换系统工具链来“修复” `python` 命令，也不要只凭 Finder 的图标判断脚本使用的解释器。命令行中的 `python3` 和 IDE 的解释器设置可能不同。

### 代码分析

```text
python3                 # 启动 REPL
python3 script.py       # 运行脚本
Control-D               # 退出 Unix 风格的 REPL
which python3           # 查看 shell 实际找到的解释器
```

- shell 先解析 `python3`，按 `PATH` 顺序查找可执行文件，再由解释器执行脚本；虚拟环境激活后，通常会把自己的 `bin` 目录放到 `PATH` 前面。
- `#!` 让可执行脚本声明解释器路径，但绝对路径会降低可移植性；`/usr/bin/env python3` 更灵活，却仍依赖目标环境的 `PATH`。

## Using Python on Linux（在 Linux 上使用 Python）

### 英文原文
> Using Python on Linux
> Python is a staple on Linux, where it’s used both for user applications and
> system tools. Because it’s so 
> ubiquitous on this Unix-based platform, Python
> may come preinstalled with your Linux distribution; type 
> python3

> window (e.g., 
> Terminal

> thing for your Linux flavor: a 
> sudo apt install python3
>  in Terminal does the
> deed in Ubuntu distributions (try 
> yum
>  on some others).

> files with the usual Terminal command lines, like those captured in 
> Figure A-11
> .
> A 
> python3
>  (or a version-specific name) starts an interactive session for typing
> and running code, and adding a filename (e.g., 
> python3 
> script
> .py
> ) runs a file.
> As on other platforms, your 
> PATH
>  setting is used by such commands to locate
> Python. As on all Unixes, the key combo Ctrl+D at 
> >>>
>  exits a Python REPL,
> and shorter shell aliases for 
> python3
>  can avoid some typing.
> 
> Figure A-11. 
> Running Python in Linux Terminal
> When using command lines, you’ll also need to use a text editor for scripts and
> modules. To make such a file of Python code, any Linux text editor will do,
> including the graphical 
> Gedit
>  default on Ubuntu and the shell-oriented 
> vi
>  and

> .

> IDLE
>  edit-and-run GUI on Linux. Launch it with an

>  command line after installing it with

> may start IDLE too.
> IDLE fine print: you may need to force versions with a more specific name (e.g.,
> idle-python3.12
> ); emojis might not work until a font install (e.g., 
> sudo apt

> ); and a platform-agnostic command
> line 
> python3 -m idlelib.idle
>  starts IDLE, too, per the Windows flavor noted
> earlier.
> Figure A-12
>  demos IDLE running on an Ubuntu Linux PC after all the kinks
> have been ironed out; it works the same on Linux as on Windows and macOS,
> and is covered in more detail in this appendix’s Windows section.
> 
> Figure A-12. 
> The IDLE GUI’s Shell window on Linux
> Also noteworthy on Linux:
> If you wish to use Python’s portable 
> tkinter
>  GUI toolkit, you can
> install it separately if needed with 
> sudo apt install python3-tk
>  (or
> similar, in the richly bifurcated world of Linux package installs).
> As on macOS, a line starting with 
> #!
>  at the top of your script can denote
> which Python runs the file, and environment variables like 
> PATH
>  and
> PYTHONPATH
>  can be set in shell startup files, but the former is not

> Chapter 22
> ’s

>  example.

> clicks
>  on Linux too, but details vary. In
> Ubuntu’s Files, “Run as a Program” runs a clicked Python file that has
> both executable permission (e.g., 
> chmod +x 
> script
> .py
> ), and a 
> #!/…
> first line that gives the path to Python, but the Windows caution about
> output disappearing on exit or error applies.
> 
> It’s not uncommon on Linux to build Python from its source code
> distribution, available at either 
> python.org
>  or GitHub. This entails a few
> simple command lines (
> configure
>  and 
> make
> ) but is beyond the scope
> of both this chapter and most Python beginners; see the Downloads
> page at 
> python.org
>  for code and details.
> For more info about Python on Linux, try the web at large or 
> python.org
> ’s
> HOWTO
> . Here, it’s 
> time to move ahead to Python’s story on mobile.

### 中文翻译

> Linux 上既有用户应用，也有系统工具使用 Python，因此 Python 是 Linux 的常见组成部分。许多发行版可能已经预装 Python；可以在终端输入 `python3` 检查。如果需要手动安装，Ubuntu 通常可以运行 `sudo apt install python3`，其他发行版使用相应的包管理器。
>
> 安装 Python 3.x 后，可以用常规 Terminal 命令启动交互会话或运行文件，例如 `python3 script.py`。命令通过 `PATH` 找到 Python；和所有 Unix 系统一样，Control+D 可以退出 REPL。编写脚本和模块时，可以使用 Ubuntu 的 Gedit，也可以使用 vi 或 nano。
>
> Linux 也可以安装 IDLE。Ubuntu 可使用 `sudo apt install idle3`，再通过 `idle` 或 `python3 -m idlelib.idle` 启动。某些发行版需要指定更具体的版本名，例如 `idle-python3.12`；显示 emoji 可能还需要字体包。`tkinter` 如果未安装，可以使用 `sudo apt install python3-tk`。
>
> Linux 脚本首行可以用 `#!` 指定解释器，`PATH` 和 `PYTHONPATH` 可以在 shell 启动文件中配置。点击运行脚本的细节取决于文件管理器；Ubuntu 需要可执行权限和正确的 `#!` 行，且仍可能遇到输出或错误信息在窗口关闭时消失的问题。
>
> 也可以从源代码构建 Python，通常涉及 `configure` 和 `make`，但这超出初学者范围。具体安装命令会随发行版变化，应查阅 Python 官方 HOWTO 和发行版文档。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：Linux 通常提供最直接的 Python 命令行环境，但发行版自带 Python 可能被系统工具依赖，项目解释器应与系统解释器隔离。
- **底层实现**：包管理器安装的解释器和模块会进入发行版管理的目录；虚拟环境通过复制或链接解释器、重写 `sys.path` 来隔离项目依赖。`python3 -m idlelib.idle` 使用当前解释器导入 IDLE，避免 `idle` 命令指向错误版本。
- **设计原因**：Unix 的文本工具、管道、权限和 shebang 机制让命令行组合很强；Python 只需遵守这些约定，就能接入已有工具链。
- **实际问题**：不要用 `sudo pip install` 污染系统环境；优先使用发行版包安装解释器、项目虚拟环境安装应用依赖，并在部署中固定依赖版本。
- **初学者误区**：`python` 可能不存在或仍指向旧版本，`pip` 也可能不对应当前解释器。始终优先使用 `python3 -m pip` 或虚拟环境中的 `python -m pip`。

### 代码分析

```bash
python3 -V
python3 -m venv .venv
. .venv/bin/activate
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
python script.py
```

- shell 负责命令查找和环境变量展开；激活虚拟环境只是改变当前 shell 的 `PATH`，并不会改变系统中其他进程的解释器。
- `python -m venv` 让指定的 Python 创建环境，随后 `python -m pip` 能保证 pip 隶属于同一解释器，避免多个 Python 并存时安装错位置。

## Using Python on Android（在 Android 上使用 Python）

### 英文原文
> Using Python on Android
> Android is a secure derivative of 
> Linux adapted for the unique constraints of
> mobile devices, and it is the most widely used operating system in the world at
> this writing. Despite this platform’s Java and Kotlin programming-language
> biases, Python can be used as a first-class programming citizen on Android
> devices in both learning and development roles. This book’s examples, for
> instance, will work well on your Android phone or tablet.
> To run Python locally on your Android, you’ll first install an app that supports it
> from an app store like 
> Play
>  or 
> F-Droid
> . Among these apps, 
> Termux
> , 
> Pydroid 3
> ,
> and 
> QPython
>  all allow you to run Python code on Android directly in multiple
> modes. While we can’t do justice to these and other Python apps, here’s a quick
> rundown of two to get you started.
> The free and open source 
> Termux
>  app for Android provides a full-featured Linux
> shell, toolset, and package manager. To use Python in Termux, first install the
> Termux app from the 
> F-Droid
>  store (its Play version is defunct). Then, open the
> app from your Apps screen, and install Python 3.X inside it with a 
> pkg install
> python
>  command line in its shell.
> Termux opens with a standard 
> Bash
>  command-line shell, where you can tap out
> commands to launch an interactive Python session with 
> python
> , and run a file of
> code by adding a filename (e.g., 
> python 
> script
> .py
> ). Stream redirection works
> as on all Unix, and command 
> python3
>  is the same as 
> python
>  if you prefer Unix
> uniformity and don’t mind the extra tap. Both commands are automatically
> usable post install without 
> PATH
>  mods.
> 
> You can use 
> code files
>  (scripts and modules) located in any folder Termux has
> access to (which generally means shared or app-private storage, per ahead) and
> make and change them either with separate text editor apps or within Termux
> itself using Linux text editors like 
> vi
>  and 
> nano
>  (install them in Termux with 
> pkg
> install
>  as needed). Termux also supports Android’s Storage Access
> Framework to make its app-private storage visible to some file-explorer apps,
> although shared storage is more accessible and usable.
> Figure A-13
>  demos the Termux app running Python code and file on Android.
> As on all Unixes, the keys combo Ctrl+D at
>  >>>
>  ends a Python interactive
> session in Termux (via Termux’s 
> CTRL
>  button or keyboards ahead), as does
> killing the app, and shell aliases can shorten the 
> python
>  (or 
> python3
> ) command.
> With apps, you’ll generally use the version of Python provided; in Termux, this
> means one of the versions in package repos, but you may be able to build a
> newer one from source code. You can also install a host of extensions to use in
> your code by command line, with both Termux’s 
> pkg
>  and Python’s 
> pip
> .
> 
> Figure A-13. 
> Running Python in the Termux app on Android
> Termux may be the path of least resistance for getting started with Python on
> Android. It has more features we’ll largely skip here, including home-screen
> widgets that run Python scripts on taps, and all Linux concepts covered earlier
> apply, including 
> PYTHONPATH
>  and 
> PATH
>  environment-variable settings. Its chief
> 
> downside for some users may be that it is limited to command lines sans its
> optional X Window System support, which is considerably complex to use;
> IDLE
> , for example, would be difficult at best to run in Termux.
> If you’re looking for something a bit more graphical, the 
> Pydroid 3
>  app also
> provides a command-line shell and interactive Python session, but it adds a GUI
> IDE for editing and launching Python code. The IDE’s edit/run window is
> captured in 
> Figure A-14
> .
> Pydroid 3 is today installed from 
> Play
> . Its shell and interactive session are less
> user-friendly than the richer command-line support in Termux, but its IDE may
> seem more comfortable for users unaccustomed to command lines. Similar in
> spirit to IDLE on PCs, this app’s IDE allows you to edit Python code, and launch
> it with a simple (and yellow) button press.
> 
> Figure A-14. 
> The Pydroid 3 app’s IDE on Android
> On top of its IDE, Pydroid 3 adds support for many popular tools, including
> scientific-programming libraries and, remarkably, Python’s 
> tkinter
>  GUI
> toolkit. As in Termux, Python’s version is preset in Pydroid 3 (though source
> builds are elusive), and Python’s 
> pip
>  is available to install extensions (though
> 
> with a dedicated GUI in this app).
> Fair warning
> : as a substantial trade-off, Pydroid 3 is also a 
> freemium
>  app, which
> will flash rude full-page ads at you unless and until you pay a required fee—an
> unfortunately common paradigm in Android, which you’ll have to weigh for
> yourself. In addition, Pydroid 3 has a history of waffling on support for 
> storage
> access in response to Android and Play edicts. By contrast, 
> Termux
>  today is
> entirely free and void of ads, supports broad storage access, and chooses
> alternative app stores rather than limiting functionality for Android changes
> mandated by Play.
> See the web and app stores for info about other Python programming apps on
> Android omitted here for space. While you’re at a store, you may also want to
> explore text editor apps like 
> QuickEdit
> —which is able to colorize and run
> Python code; and alternative onscreen keyboards like 
> Hacker’s Keyboard
> —
> which adds PC keys not available in stock options but commonly used for
> coding (e.g., arrows and Ctrl). Some Python apps also include tools to augment
> onscreen keyboards that can be tailored or disabled, and Bluetooth keyboards
> and casting to larger screens can naturally aid usability too.
> It’s also worth noting in closing that 
> CPython
>  plans to add Android to its list of
> officially supported platforms soon, which may foster additional options going
> forward. Moreover, although most beginners will use an app to run Python code
> on Android as described, it’s also possible to build standalone apps for Android
> that are coded in Python but used like any other app. We’ll return to this option
> at the end of this appendix after one last platform.
> ANDROID’S PROPRIETARY WORLD
> Python programmers should also be aware that Android imposes numerous
> constraints on apps, some of which may seem onerous to developers with
> backgrounds in more interoperable platforms. Most of these constraints are
> rationalized on the grounds of security or performance, but all reduce utility.
> Android’s 
> storage
> , for instance, is split into a shared and persistent area with
> controlled access, along with areas partly or wholly private to apps that may
> vaporize on app uninstalls. Hence, while POSIX file tools and paths do work
> on Android, Python code must take care to either use accessible folders or
> 
> run proprietary Java API tools that request or use enhanced permissions.
> In addition, background or long-running 
> processes
>  may run afoul of limits;
> opinionated choices of 
> tools and languages
>  are nearly imposed on
> developers; and 
> throttling
>  for power, heat, memory, or other bias is a norm
> on most phones.
> On the upside, Android users can install apps outside its owner’s store and
> can access the filesystem with numerous file-explorer apps. That makes
> Android more open than iOS today, but this is a large and fluid topic. If you
> care about using Python on mobiles, be sure 
> to watch other resources for
> news on this front.

### 中文翻译

> Android 是针对移动设备独特约束改造的安全 Linux 衍生系统，在本书写作时是世界上使用最广泛的操作系统。尽管 Android 偏向 Java 和 Kotlin，Python 仍然可以作为一等公民用于学习和开发，本书示例也能很好地在 Android 手机或平板上运行。
>
> 在 Android 本地运行 Python，首先需要从 Play 或 F-Droid 等应用商店安装支持 Python 的应用。Termux、Pydroid 3 和 QPython 都能以多种方式直接运行 Python。Termux 是免费开源的完整 Linux shell、工具集和包管理器；从 F-Droid 安装后，在 Termux 中运行 `pkg install python`，再用 `python` 或 `python script.py` 启动交互会话或脚本。
>
> Termux 可以访问它有权限的共享或应用私有目录，也可以使用 vi、nano 等 Linux 编辑器编辑代码。Control+D 可以退出 REPL。Termux 还支持 `pkg` 和 Python 的 `pip` 安装扩展，但 IDLE 等 GUI 在 Termux 中需要复杂的 X Window 配置。
>
> Pydroid 3 同样提供 shell 和交互式 Python，并增加了编辑和运行代码的 GUI IDE。它支持很多科学计算库和 `tkinter`，还提供图形化 pip；代价是它采用 freemium 模式，免费版本可能显示广告，并且存储访问策略会随 Android 和 Play 的要求变化。Termux 免费、无广告，存储访问也更开放，但主要面向命令行。
>
> Android 还存在应用存储沙盒、后台进程限制、权限申请、工具链偏好和功耗/温度/内存节流等约束。POSIX 路径和文件工具仍可用，但代码必须使用可访问目录，或者调用申请增强权限的专有 Java API。另一方面，Android 允许从官方商店之外安装应用，并可通过文件管理器访问文件，因此目前比 iOS 更开放。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：Android 上的“能运行 Python”与“能像桌面一样运行任意 Python 程序”是两件事。应用沙盒、存储权限、后台限制和 CPU 架构会改变程序可用的库和行为。
- **底层实现**：Termux 在应用中提供 Linux 用户空间；Pydroid 3 把解释器和 IDE 打包进应用。两者的 Python、扩展二进制和文件权限都受宿主应用及 Android API 约束。
- **设计原因**：移动系统用沙盒和后台限制换取安全、电量和性能可控；这会牺牲传统桌面程序假设的全局文件系统和长期后台进程。
- **实际问题**：移动部署要显式选择存储目录、申请权限、处理应用暂停和进程终止，并测试目标 Android 版本；不要只在桌面 Linux 上验证文件路径和线程行为。
- **初学者误区**：安装了 Python 应用不代表可以直接安装所有 PyPI 包；需要 C 编译器、系统库或 GUI 后端的扩展可能无法在手机上构建或运行。

### 代码分析

```bash
pkg install python
python
python script.py
python -m pip install package_name
```

- `pkg` 是 Termux 的包管理器，先安装应用可用的 Python；随后 `pip` 只在该解释器和应用环境中管理扩展。
- 文件路径和权限由 Android 应用沙盒决定。代码使用 `os.getcwd()` 或 `pathlib.Path` 检查实际工作目录，比硬编码桌面路径更可靠。

## Using Python on iOS（在 iOS 上使用 Python）

### 英文原文
> Using Python on iOS
> iOS—which includes its iPadOS offshoot in this guide—is a macOS derivative
> targeted at mobile devices. Like Android, it 
> has limiting biases for programming
> languages (Swift and Objective-C), and it is even more strict about carving up
> storage into restricted app sandboxes with proprietary access rules and tools.
> Despite these constraints, though, your iPhone or iPad can be used to run Python
> code, too, including the code in this book.
> Like Android, you’ll normally use Python on your iOS devices by installing an
> app that runs Python code. Among these, 
> Pythonista 3
>  provides an interactive
> Python session and a GUI for editing and running files of code, as in other IDEs.
> In addition, this app comes with access to native iOS features and a toolkit for
> building GUIs in Python for iOS. It also can share code files with the 
> Files
>  app
> to be opened with taps.
> To vet for yourself, fetch Pythonista 3 from the 
> App Store
> . 
> Figure A-15
>  shows
> this app in action (yes, on a humble and historical iPod). Be sure to also explore
> the other iOS options on the store; the 
> Pyto
>  app, for example, provides similar
> functionality
> , and comes with the 
> Toga
>  UI library for coding portable GUIs
> (there’s more on Toga at standalone apps ahead).
> 
> Figure A-15. 
> Running Python in the Pythonista 3 app on iOS
> Apart from app choices and platform restrictions, using Python on an iOS device
> is largely the same as on Android and PCs, so we’ll skip further details here. For
> more on using Python for iPhone and iPad, see the Apple App Store and the web
> at large.
> Like Android, iOS is also scheduled to be granted officially supported status in
> CPython
>  soon, which may yield options impossible to predict today; watch the
> web for new developments. Also like Android, it’s possible to package your
> Python programs as standalone apps for iOS, but 
> we must move on to this
> appendix’s next section to see how.

### 中文翻译

> iOS（本指南也包括 iPadOS）是面向移动设备的 macOS 衍生系统。和 Android 一样，它对编程语言有偏好，主要是 Swift 和 Objective-C；它把存储划分到更严格的应用沙盒中，并使用专有的访问规则和工具。即便如此，iPhone 和 iPad 也可以运行 Python，包括本书中的代码。
>
> 通常需要安装能运行 Python 的应用。`Pythonista 3` 提供交互式 Python 会话、编辑和运行文件的 GUI，并能访问原生 iOS 功能，还提供用 Python 构建 iOS GUI 的工具包；代码文件也可以与 Files 应用共享。`Pyto` 提供类似能力，并带有用于可移植 GUI 的 Toga UI 库。
>
> 除了应用选择和平台限制，在 iOS 设备上使用 Python 的方式大体与 Android 和个人电脑相同。未来 CPython 可能正式支持 Android 和 iOS，但具体形式在当前无法预测，应以最新文档和应用商店信息为准。Python 程序也可以打包为 iOS 独立应用。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：iOS 上的 Python 通常是应用内解释器，而不是可自由访问系统的 CPython 安装。应用沙盒是程序设计的一部分，不能用桌面路径、全局后台进程等假设。
- **底层实现**：Pythonista、Pyto 等应用把解释器、编辑器和平台桥接封装在自己的权限范围内；GUI 和原生能力通过应用提供的 API 暴露，而不是标准库自动获得。
- **设计原因**：iOS 的严格签名、沙盒和存储政策以安全和应用审核为优先，因此可移植 Python 代码需要适配受限文件系统和生命周期。
- **实际问题**：将代码放入 Files 或应用允许的目录，避免依赖任意绝对路径；需要分发时评估应用签名、商店规则、原生依赖和离线行为。
- **初学者误区**：在 iOS 应用里可以运行脚本，不代表可以用 `pip` 安装任意桌面扩展，也不代表脚本拥有启动后台服务或访问所有文件的权限。

### 代码分析

```python
from pathlib import Path

data_file = Path.home() / 'Documents' / 'data.txt'
data_file.write_text('hello', encoding='utf-8')
print(data_file.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
```

- `Path` 只负责构造路径和读写请求；最终能否访问由 iOS 宿主应用授予的沙盒目录决定。
- 指定 UTF-8 让文本结果不依赖平台默认编码，但无法绕过权限、文件共享和应用生命周期限制。

## Standalone Apps and Executables（独立应用与可执行文件）

### 英文原文
> Standalone Apps and Executables
> Besides running Python source code with the traditional schemes we’ve just met,
> it’s also possible to bundle Python code 
> into a standalone program that users run
> the same way they run any other program on their device (e.g., by a click or tap).
> In fact, users can’t even tell these bundles are written in Python at all: no source
> code is visible, no other installs or apps are required, and changes in a locally
> installed Python have no effect on the bundle.
> The way you’ll build standalones varies per platform. As a noncomprehensive
> sample of prominent tools today, you can build standalone executables for
> Windows and Linux with 
> PyInstaller
> ; standalone apps for macOS with
> PyInstaller
>  and 
> py2app
> ; and standalone apps for Android and iOS with
> Buildozer
>  and 
> Briefcase
>  (the latter also offers options for PCs).
> On Android, for instance, it’s possible to develop standalone apps completely in
> Python. Although this takes more effort than running code in another app, its
> products are fully functional and idiomatic GUI apps coded in Python, which run
> with a tap, leverage Android APIs when needed, and can be both side-loaded
> and uploaded to app stores.
> As a demo, 
> Figure A-16
>  captures one of many Python-coded standalone apps for
> Android, running on a foldable. This app, made by this book’s author, is freely
> available in the Play store; is built for Android with 
> Buildozer
> ; uses the 
> portable
> Kivy
>  toolkit for its GUI; and relies on Kivy’s 
> pyjnius
>  to access Android Java
> 
> APIs when required in a small fraction of its code (e.g., to request permissions,
> run services, open docs, and get drive labels).
> Figure A-16. 
> A Python-coded standalone app running on Android
> Crucially, such apps can also run on PC platforms—Windows, macOS, and
> Linux—from 
> the same code base
> . 
> Figure A-17
> , for instance, shows the same app
> 
> running on macOS. Its code is bundled for macOS and other PCs with
> PyInstaller
> ; its Kivy GUI is automatically cross-platform; and its POSIX file-
> sync code works everywhere. The net result is a Python-coded app that runs
> across a range of PC and mobile hosts, with native behavior on each.
> To see this for yourself, fetch this app’s Android version on Play and its PC
> versions at this book’s website or 
> quixotely.com
> . Disclaimer: if the Android app
> is unavailable on Play, check for it at the latter two sites or try a web search;
> book lifespans tend to be substantially longer than those of apps dependent on
> stores and platforms (see Termux’s troubles!).
> Figure A-17. 
> The same app running on macOS
> Nor is this toolset the only interoperability game in town. The alternative
> BeeWare
> , with its 
> portable 
> Toga
>  GUI toolkit and 
> Briefcase
>  app builder, promises
> similar platform independence and advertises additional packaging options on
> PCs. Moreover, some apps built with such tools can work on iOS, too, though its
> lack of a user-accessible filesystem renders much cross-platform code unusable
> 
> (e.g., POSIX file-path syncs are impossible).
> The takeaway here: with a portable programming tool like Python, you’re not
> locked into a single platform’s proprietary realm—unless, that is, you develop
> for platforms that disqualify code that runs anywhere else. As always, choose
> your coding battles wisely. Security counts, but closed platforms enable
> monopolies and stifle innovation.
> Standalones may not be very useful when you’re just getting started with Python
> (and make no sense at all for running the examples in this book!), but they may
> become more important when you start writing programs for others to use. When
> you’re ready to explore standalone deliverables in Python, see the web for
> current tools and details in this domain.

### 中文翻译

> 除了前面介绍的传统运行方式，还可以把 Python 代码打包成独立程序，让用户像运行其他程序一样通过单击或轻触启动。用户甚至不必知道这些程序由 Python 编写：包内不暴露源代码，不要求用户另行安装 Python，机器上安装的 Python 发生变化也不会影响它。
>
> 独立程序的构建方式取决于平台。举例来说，Windows 和 Linux 可以使用 PyInstaller；macOS 可以使用 PyInstaller 或 py2app；Android 和 iOS 可以使用 Buildozer 和 Briefcase，后者也支持个人电脑。Android 上可以完全用 Python 开发可通过点击运行、调用 Android API、侧载或上传应用商店的 GUI 应用。
>
> 作者以 Kivy 和 Buildozer 构建的 Android 应用为例：GUI 使用可移植的 Kivy，必要时通过 pyjnius 访问 Android Java API。相同代码库也可以用 PyInstaller 打包到 Windows、macOS 和 Linux；Kivy GUI 与 POSIX 文件同步代码因此可以在多种主机上运行。
>
> BeeWare 是另一套方案，提供可移植的 Toga GUI 工具包和 Briefcase 打包器。不过 iOS 没有用户可访问的通用文件系统，某些跨平台代码（例如 POSIX 路径同步）在那里无法工作。总体而言，Python 可以减少对单一专有平台的锁定，但仍要根据安全、平台政策和互操作性选择开发目标。
>
> 刚开始学习 Python 时不需要独立打包，本书示例也不适合这样运行；当你开始为他人交付程序时，它才会变得重要。届时应查阅当前工具和平台文档，因为打包工具和应用商店政策变化很快。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：独立打包解决的是“交付和启动体验”，不是自动解决代码兼容、原生依赖、性能或安全问题。它把解释器、标准库和依赖放进发布物，换来更大的体积和更复杂的构建链。
- **底层实现**：PyInstaller 等工具通常收集字节码、Python 运行时和扩展库，生成目录或单文件启动器；移动工具还需要生成符合平台签名、权限和生命周期要求的应用包。
- **设计原因**：用户不必安装 Python，能降低桌面和手机软件的分发门槛；可移植 GUI 工具则把界面抽象到多个宿主平台。
- **实际问题**：构建时固定目标平台、架构和依赖版本，扫描包内许可证和敏感文件，并在干净机器上测试安装、升级、数据路径、权限和卸载行为。
- **初学者误区**：独立可执行文件不等于源码不可逆，也不意味着所有动态导入都会被自动发现；打包后应重点测试插件、资源文件、子进程和原生扩展。

### 代码分析

```bash
python -m pip install pyinstaller
pyinstaller --onefile app.py
```

- 打包器先分析导入图并收集运行时文件，再生成启动器；动态构造的模块名、运行时读取的资源和外部命令可能需要额外配置。
- `--onefile` 方便分发但启动时可能先解包到临时目录；目录模式通常更易诊断缺少文件的问题。选择应由交付约束决定，而不是只看文件数量。

## Etcetera（其他选项）

### 英文原文
> Etcetera
> While the platform techniques we’ve explored here are perhaps the simplest and
> most common ways to use Python, there’s much more to this story. For instance,
> this appendix hasn’t said anything about using Python in:
> Other IDEs like 
> PyCharm
> , 
> PyDev
> , 
> Wing
> , and

> Web-based notebooks like 
> IPython
>  and 
> Jupyter
> Alternative Python implementations like 
> PyPy
> , 
> Cython
> , 
> Numba
> , and
> Jython
> Alternative Python distributions like 
> Anaconda
>  and 
> ActiveState
> The cells and macros of spreadsheets like 
> Excel
> Web servers using frameworks like 
> Flask
>  and 
> Django
> Web browsers using the emerging 
> WebAssembly
>  and 
> Pyodide
> And lots of other options in no way judged by omission here. This book visits
> some of these in 
> Chapter 1
> , summarizes Python implementations in 
> Chapter 2
> ,
> briefly reviews 
> Jupyter
>  and 
> WebAssembly
>  in 
> Chapter 3
> , and uses 
> PyPy
>  for
> benchmarks in 
> Chapter 21
> . In general, though, advanced usage contexts like
> these are interesting but out of scope for this Python fundamentals text, and best
> 
> deferred until you’ve mastered the language itself.
> In the end, Python usage details and options tend to evolve as rapidly as Python
> itself. Indeed, each prior edition of this book has had to revise its usage coverage
> radically, and this one expects to fare no better. As noted at the start of this
> appendix, you should expect to 
> check both Python’s docs and the web at large
> for new-and-exciting developments almost certain to emerge by the time you
> read these words.

### 中文翻译

> 前面介绍的平台技术可能是最简单、最常见的 Python 使用方式，但这并不是全部。例如，本附录没有展开 PyCharm、PyDev、Wing 和 VSCode 等其他 IDE；IPython 和 Jupyter 等基于 Web 的 notebook；PyPy、Cython、Numba 和 Jython 等替代实现；Anaconda 和 ActiveState 等发行版；Excel 等电子表格的单元格和宏；Flask 和 Django 等 Web 框架；以及 WebAssembly 和 Pyodide 等浏览器方案。
>
> 这并不是因为遗漏就否定其他选项。本书在第 1 章提到其中一些工具，在第 2 章概括 Python 实现，在第 3 章简要介绍 Jupyter 和 WebAssembly，并在第 21 章用 PyPy 做基准测试。不过，对 Python 基础教材来说，这些高级场景超出范围，最好等掌握语言本身后再深入。
>
> Python 的使用细节和选项与 Python 语言本身一样快速变化。本书每一版都不得不大幅修改相关内容，因此读者应预期查阅 Python 文档和整个 Web，寻找本书出版后很可能出现的新发展。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：生态选择有层次：解释器、发行版、编辑器、notebook、Web 框架和打包工具分别解决不同问题，不能用“最流行”替代需求分析。
- **底层实现**：CPython、PyPy、Cython、Numba 等在执行模型、扩展接口和性能特征上不同；WebAssembly/Pyodide 还增加了浏览器沙盒和异步 I/O 边界。
- **设计原因**：附录只提供入门路径，是为了避免把工具生态误当成语言基础。先掌握标准 Python，再根据瓶颈选择实现或平台，迁移成本更低。
- **实际问题**：评估工具时记录版本、维护状态、许可证、原生依赖、部署目标和社区支持；对关键路径保留替代方案，并用最小样例验证性能和可移植性。
- **初学者误区**：IDE、发行版和 Python 解释器不是同一个概念；安装一个大而全的工具包也不等于理解环境。工具越多，越要确认当前命令实际使用的是哪个解释器。

## Appendix B. Solutions to End-of-Part Exercises（附录 B：各部分末尾练习答案）

### 英文原文
> Appendix B. 
> Solutions to End-of-
> Part Exercises
> This appendix provides solutions for the book’s end-of-part exercises. Code files
> named by captions or narrative in these solutions are available in the book
> examples package’s 
> AppendixB
>  folder, which has one subfolder per part (e.g.,
> AppendixB/Part1
>  is the first part’s files). See the 
> Preface
>  for more info on the
> examples package.

### 中文翻译

> 本附录提供本书各部分末尾练习的答案。答案中按标题或叙述引用的代码文件，均可在本书示例包的 `AppendixB` 文件夹中找到；该文件夹按部分划分子目录，例如 `AppendixB/Part1` 存放第一部分的文件。更多信息请参见前言中的示例包说明。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：附录 B 是练习的可执行参考答案，不是新的语言章节。它把前面分散的语法、对象、函数、模块、类和异常知识放回完整程序中。
- **底层实现**：答案中的交互会经过 REPL 的解析、编译和执行；脚本会建立模块命名空间并可能生成字节码缓存；异常示例则展示调用栈如何形成 traceback。
- **设计原因**：按 Part 分组能让读者只查阅已经学习过的知识，也能通过代码文件名和示例编号定位可运行材料。
- **实际问题**：运行答案时应以当前 Python 版本和操作系统为准，先在隔离目录中执行，检查路径、编码、权限和第三方依赖；示例中的硬编码路径和账号必须替换。
- **初学者误区**：答案是参考实现而非唯一实现；能得到同样输出不表示理解了名称绑定、可变对象、导入缓存或异常传播。

> Errors and debugging
> : The following 
> interaction demonstrates the sorts
> of error messages you’ll get when you complete this exercise. Really,
> you’re triggering Python exceptions; the default exception-handling
> behavior terminates the running Python program and prints an error
> message and stack trace on the screen. The stack trace shows where you
> were in a program when the exception occurred (if function calls are
> active when the error happens, the “Traceback” section displays all
> active call levels).
> In 
> Chapter 10
>  and 
> Part VII
> , you will learn that you can catch exceptions
> using

>  statements and process them arbitrarily. You’ll also learn that
> Python includes a full-blown source code debugger (module 
> pdb
> ) for
> special error-detection requirements. For now, notice that Python gives

> crashing silently:
> $ 
> python3
> >>> 
> 2 ** 500
> 32733906078961418700131896968275991522166420460430647894832913680961337964
> 046745

> >>>

> oops
> Traceback (most recent call last):
>  File "<stdin>", line 1, in <module>
> NameError: name 'oops' is not defined
> 6
> . 
> Breaks and cycles
> : When you type 
> this code:
> $ 
> python3
> >>> 
> L = [1, 2]
> >>> 
> L.append(L)
> >>> 
> L
> [1, 2, [...]]
> you create a 
> cyclic
>  data 
> structure in Python. In Python releases before
> 1.5.1, the Python printer wasn’t smart enough to detect cycles in
> objects, and it would print an unending stream of 
> [1, 2, [1, 2, [1,
> 2, [1, 2
> , and so on until you hit the Ctrl+C break-key combination on
> your machine (which, technically, raises a keyboard-interrupt exception
> that prints a default message). Beginning with Python 1.5.1, the printer
> is clever enough to detect cycles, prints 
> [[...]]
>  instead to let you
> know that it has detected a loop in the object’s structure, and avoids
> getting stuck printing forever.
> The reason for the cycle is subtle and requires information you will
> glean in 
> Part II
> , so this is something of a preview. But in short,
> assignments in Python always generate 
> references
>  to objects, not copies
> of them. You can think of objects as chunks of memory and of
> references as implicitly followed pointers. When you run the first
> assignment in the preceding code, the name 
> L
>  becomes a named
> reference to a two-item list object—a pointer to a piece of memory.
> Python lists are really arrays of object references, with an 
> append
> method that changes the array in place by tacking on another object
> reference at the end. Here, the 
> append
>  call adds a reference to the front
> of 
> L
>  at the end of 
> L
> , which leads to the cycle illustrated in 
> Figure B-1
> : a
> 
> pointer at the end of the list that points back to the front of the list.
> Figure B-1. 
> A cyclic object, created by appending a list to itself
> Besides being printed specially, as you’ll learn in 
> Chapter 6
> , cyclic
> objects must also be handled specially by Python’s garbage collector, or
> their space will remain unreclaimed even when they are no longer in
> use. Though rare in practice, in some programs that traverse arbitrary
> objects or structures, you might have to detect such cycles yourself by
> keeping track of where you’ve been to avoid looping. Believe it or not,
> cyclic data structures can sometimes be useful, despite their special-case
> printing.

### 中文翻译

> 请参阅第 3 章中的“知识测验：第一部分练习”。
>
> **1. 交互**：假定 Python 已正确配置，交互会话应类似示例中的形式。你可以在 IDLE、控制台、应用或 notebook 页面中运行它：启动 `python3`，在 `>>>` 提示符下输入表达式，解释器会回显 `'Hello World!'`。在 Unix 上使用 Ctrl+D、Windows 上使用 Ctrl+Z 后回车退出，也可以直接关闭窗口。
>
> **2. 程序**：代码文件（也就是模块）可以只包含 `print('Hello module world!')`。使用当前平台的 Python 命令运行 `python3 module1.py`，会输出 `Hello module world!`。也可以双击文件、使用 IDLE 的 `Run -> Run Module`，或通过 notebook/IDE 的界面运行。
>
> **3. 模块**：在交互解释器中执行 `import module1` 会运行模块的顶层代码并输出消息。再次运行前需要重新加载模块，或者重启 REPL。移动 `.py` 文件后，原目录中的 `__pycache__` 字节码缓存不会自动成为新目录中的可导入模块；导入仍取决于源文件或缓存文件是否位于 Python 的搜索路径。`compileall -b -l .` 可以生成直接放在源文件旁边的旧式 `.pyc` 文件。
>
> **4. 脚本**：如果平台支持 `#!`，脚本可以在首行指定 Python 路径，然后通过执行权限和 `./script1.py` 运行，也可以用更可移植的 `python3 script1.py` 运行。`#!` 方案是可选的，在不同机器上的绝对路径可能不同。
>
> **5. 错误与调试**：除非程序捕获异常，否则 Python 会终止当前程序，打印错误消息和 traceback。traceback 展示异常发生的位置和活动的调用层级。示例中的 `2 ** 500` 可以得到大整数，`1 / 0` 会产生 `ZeroDivisionError`，输入未定义名称会产生 `NameError`。这些消息比静默崩溃更有助于定位问题；后续章节会介绍 `try` 和 `pdb`。
>
> **6. 中断与循环**：执行 `L = [1, 2]` 后再执行 `L.append(L)`，会把列表自身作为元素添加进去，形成循环数据结构。现代 Python 的打印器会把循环显示为 `[1, 2, [...]]`，而不是无限输出。原因是赋值总是建立对对象的引用，不会自动复制列表；列表内部保存的是对象引用，`append` 又在原列表上修改它。垃圾回收器也必须特殊处理这种循环，否则对象不再被外部使用时仍可能占据内存。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：第一部分答案从“如何启动代码”逐步推进到“模块如何导入、错误如何传播、对象如何共享引用”，强调 Python 程序是由解释器环境、文件和对象关系共同构成的。
- **底层实现**：REPL 和脚本都会先把源代码解析并编译成字节码；导入会创建模块对象并缓存到 `sys.modules`；列表保存指向对象的引用，循环引用由垃圾回收器的循环检测处理。
- **设计原因**：Python 让交互、脚本、模块和 notebook 共享同一语言模型，降低实验到程序化的转换成本；明确显示 traceback 则把运行时错误变成可定位信息。
- **实际问题**：排查导入问题应检查当前工作目录、`sys.path`、`sys.modules` 和 `__pycache__`，排查内存问题则要识别长生命周期容器中的引用和循环；不要只看文件是否存在。
- **初学者误区**：`import` 不是每次都重新执行模块，`L2 = L` 也不是复制列表；`.pyc` 不是独立替代搜索路径的万能缓存，异常 traceback 也不是错误发生前的完整历史日志。

> 2
> . 
> Indexing and slicing
> : Indexing out of 
> bounds (e.g., 
> L[4]
> ) raises an
> error; Python always checks to make sure that all offsets are within the
> bounds of a sequence.
> On the other hand, slicing out of bounds (e.g., 
> L[-1000:100]
> ) works
> because Python scales out-of-bounds slices so that they always fit (the
> limits are set to zero and the sequence length, if required).
> Extracting a sequence in reverse, with the lower bound greater than the
> higher bound (e.g., 
> L[3:1]
> ), doesn’t really work. You get back an
> empty slice (
> []
> ) because Python scales the slice limits to make sure that
> the lower bound is always less than or equal to the upper bound (e.g.,
> L[3:1]
>  is scaled to 
> L[3:3]
> , the empty insertion point at offset 
> 3
> ).
> Python slices are always extracted from left to right, even if you use
> negative indexes (they are first converted to positive indexes by adding
> the sequence length). Note that Python’s three-limit slices modify this
> behavior somewhat. For instance, 
> L[3:1:-1]
>  does extract from right to
> left:
> 
> >>> 
> L = [1, 2, 3, 4]
> >>> 
> L[4]
> Traceback (most recent call last):
>  File "<stdin>", line 1, in <module>
> IndexError: list index out of range
> >>> 
> L[-1000:100]
> [1, 2, 3, 4]
> >>> 
> L[3:1]
> []
> >>> 
> L
> [1, 2, 3, 4]
> >>> 
> L[3:1] = ['?']
> >>> 
> L
> [1, 2, 3, '?', 4]
> 3
> . 
> Indexing, slicing, and 
> del
> : Your interaction with the interpreter should
> look something like the following. Note that assigning an empty list to
> an offset stores an empty list object there, but assigning an empty list to
> a slice deletes the slice. Slice assignment expects another sequence, or
> you’ll get a type error; it inserts items 
> inside
>  the sequence assigned, not
> the sequence itself:
> >>> 
> L = [1, 2, 3, 4]
> >>> 
> L[2] = []
> >>> 
> L
> [1, 2, [], 4]
> >>> 
> L[2:3] = []
> >>> 
> L
> [1, 2, 4]
> >>> 
> del L[0]
> >>> 
> L
> 
> [2, 4]
> >>> 
> del L[1:]
> >>> 
> L
> [2]
> >>> 
> L[1:2] = 1
> Traceback (most recent call last):
>  File "<stdin>", line 1, in <module>
> TypeError: can only assign an iterable
> 4
> . 
> Tuple assignment
> : The values of 
> X
>  and 
> Y
>  are swapped. When tuples
> appear 
> on the left and right of an assignment symbol (
> =
> ), Python assigns
> objects on the right to targets on the left according to their positions.
> This is probably easiest to understand by noting that the targets on the
> left aren’t a real tuple, even though they look like one; they are simply a
> set of independent assignment targets. The items on the right are a tuple,
> which gets unpacked during the assignment (this tuple provides the
> temporary assignment needed to achieve the swap effect):
> >>> 
> X = 'code'
> >>> 
> Y = 'hack'
> >>> 
> X, Y = Y, X
> >>> 
> X
> 'hack'
> >>> 
> Y
> 'code'
> 5
> . 
> Dictionary keys
> : Any 
> immutable
>  (technically, “hashable”) object can be
> used as a 
> dictionary key, including integers, tuples, strings, and so on.
> This really is a dictionary, even though some of its keys look like
> integer offsets. Mixed-type keys work fine, too:
> >>> 
> D = {}
> >>> 
> D[1] = 'a'
> >>> 
> D[2] = 'b'
> 
> >>> 
> D[(1, 2, 3)] = 'c'
> >>> 
> D
> {1: 'a', 2: 'b', (1, 2, 3): 'c'}
> 6
> . 
> Dictionary indexing
> : Indexing a nonexistent key (
> D['d']
> ) raises an
> error; assigning 
> to a nonexistent key (
> D['d']='hack'
> ) creates a new
> dictionary entry. On the other hand, out-of-bounds indexing for lists
> raises an error, too, but so do out-of-bounds assignments. Variable
> names work like dictionary keys; they must have already been assigned
> when referenced, but they are created when first assigned. In fact,
> variable names can be processed as dictionary keys if you wish (they’re
> made visible in the dictionaries of stack frames or module [or other
> object] namespaces):
> >>> 
> D = {'a': 1, 'b': 2, 'c': 3}
> >>> 
> D['a']
> 1
> >>> 
> D['d']
> Traceback (most recent call last):
>  File "<stdin>", line 1, in <module>
> KeyError: 'd'
> >>> 
> D['d'] = 4
> >>> 
> D
> {'a': 1, 'b': 2, 'c': 3, 'd': 4}
> >>> 
> L = [0, 1]
> >>> 
> L[2]
> Traceback (most recent call last):
>  File "<stdin>", line 1, in <module>
> IndexError: list index out of range
> >>> 
> L[2] = 3
> Traceback (most recent call last):
>  File "<stdin>", line 1, in <module>
> IndexError: list assignment index out of range
> 
> 7
> . 
> Generic operations
> : Question answers (with some error text omitted in
> listings):
> a
> . 
> The 
> +
>  operator 
> doesn’t work on different/mixed types (e.g.,
> string 
> +
>  list, list 
> +
>  tuple).
> b
> . 
> +
>  doesn’t work for dictionaries, as they aren’t sequences
> (though 
> |
>  does).
> c
> . 
> The 
> append
>  method works only for lists, not strings, and 
> keys
> works only on dictionaries. 
> append
>  assumes its target is
> mutable, since it’s an in-place extension; strings are immutable.
> Dictionary 
> keys
>  is similarly type specific.
> d
> . 
> Slicing and concatenation always return a new object of the
> same type as the objects processed:
> >>> 
> 'x' + 1
> TypeError: illegal argument type for built-in operation
> >>> 
> {} + {}
> TypeError: bad operand type(s) for +
> >>> 
> [].append(9)
> >>> 
> ''.append('s')
> AttributeError: attribute-less object
> >>> 
> list({}.keys())
> []
> >>> 
> [].keys()
> AttributeError: keys
> >>> 
> [][:]
> []
> >>> 
> ''[:]
> ''
> 
> 8
> . 
> String indexing
> : This is a bit of a 
> trick question—because strings are
> collections of one-character strings, every time you index a string, you
> get back a string that can be indexed again. 
> S[0][0][0][0][0]
>  just
> keeps indexing the first character over and over. This generally doesn’t
> work for lists (lists can hold arbitrary objects) unless the list contains
> strings:
> >>> 
> S = 'hack'
> >>> 
> S[0][0][0][0][0]
> 'h'
> >>> 
> L = ['h', 'a']
> >>> 
> L[0][0][0]
> 'h'
> 9
> . 
> Immutable types
> : Either of 
> the following solutions works. Index
> assignment doesn’t because strings are immutable:
> >>> 
> S = 'hack'
> >>> 
> S = S[0] + 'e' + S[2:]
> >>> 
> S
> 'heck' 
> >>> 
> S = S[0] + 'i' + S[2] + S[3]
> >>> 
> S
> 'hick'
> (See also the 
> bytearray
>  string type in 
> Chapter 37
> —it’s a mutable
> sequence of small integers that is essentially processed the same as a
> string, especially when its bytes are ASCII character code points.)
> 10
> . 
> Nesting
> : Here is a 
> sample (your specs will vary):
> >>> 
> pat = {'name': ('Pat', 'Q', 'Jones'), 'age': None, 'job': 'engineer'}
> >>> 
> pat['job']
> 'engineer'
> >>> 
> pat['name'][2]
> 'Jones'
> 
> 11
> . 
> Files
> : Examples B-3 and B-4 show one way to create and read back a
> text file in Python using Unicode encoding defaults on the host (which
> are generally moot for simple ASCII text like this):
> Example B-3. 
> Part2/maker.py
> file = open('myfile.txt', 'w')
> file.write('Hello file world!\n') 
> # Or: open().write()
> file.close() 
> # close not always needed
> Example B-4. 
> Part2/reader.py
> file = open('myfile.txt') 
> # 'r' is default open mode
> print(file.read()) 
> # Or print(open().read())
> When run (here, from a console command line), the file shows up in the
> directory you’re working in because its name has no path prefix. The 
> ls
> here is a Unix command; use 
> dir
>  on Windows:
> $ 
> python3 maker.py
> $ 
> python3 reader.py
> Hello file world!
> $ 
> ls -l myfile.txt
> -rw-r--r-- 1 me staff 18 Aug 11 19:34 myfile.txt
> 
### 中文翻译

> 请参阅第 15 章中的“知识测验：第三部分练习”。
>
> **1. 基础操作**：示例展示数字、字符串、元组、列表、字典和空值对象的结果。分号可以把多条语句放在一行，逗号会创建显示为括号的元组。`2 ** 16` 得到 65536，`2 / 5` 和 `2 / 5.0` 得到 `(0.4, 0.4)`；字符串支持拼接、重复、索引、切片以及 `%`、`str.format` 和 f-string 格式化。列表支持拼接、切片、索引、原地 `reverse()` 和 `sort()`；字典按键访问、添加条目、使用不可变元组作为键，并提供 `keys()`、`values()` 和成员测试。
>
> **2. 索引与切片**：序列索引越界会报错，但越界切片会被调整到合法范围。`L[3:1]` 得到空切片，因为默认步长为正；带负步长时如 `L[3:1:-1]` 才会从右向左提取。切片赋值可以改变序列长度，插入的是右侧序列中的元素。
>
> **3. 索引、切片和 `del`**：给单个列表位置赋空列表会存储一个空列表对象；给切片赋空列表则会删除该切片。`del L[0]` 删除一个元素，`del L[1:]` 删除剩余部分。切片赋值要求右侧是可迭代对象，直接写 `L[1:2] = 1` 会产生 `TypeError`。
>
> **4. 元组赋值**：`X, Y = Y, X` 会交换两个名称绑定的对象。右侧先构成元组并在赋值时解包，左侧只是两个独立的赋值目标，不是一个真正需要修改的元组。
>
> **5. 字典键**：任何不可变、可哈希对象都可以作为字典键，包括整数、字符串和元组；不同类型的键可以共存。看起来像整数偏移的键仍然只是普通字典键。
>
> **6. 字典索引**：读取不存在的键会产生 `KeyError`，给不存在的键赋值则会创建新条目。列表的越界读取和越界赋值都会产生 `IndexError`。变量名也可以理解为命名空间字典中的键，但引用前必须已经绑定。
>
> **7. 通用操作**：不同类型之间不能随意使用 `+`；字符串加列表、列表加元组以及两个字典都不支持，字典可以使用 `|` 合并。`append` 只适用于列表，`keys` 只适用于字典；切片和拼接通常返回与输入同类型的新对象。
>
> **8. 字符串索引**：字符串是由单字符字符串组成的集合，因此 `S[0][0][0]` 仍然可以继续索引并得到首字符。列表可以保存任意对象，只有当列表元素本身是字符串时，类似的连续索引才会成立。
>
> **9. 不可变类型**：字符串不能通过索引赋值修改。要把 `'hack'` 改成 `'heck'` 或 `'hick'`，应构造新字符串并重新绑定 `S`。`bytearray` 是可变的小整数序列，在处理 ASCII 字节时可作为可变替代方案。
>
> **10. 嵌套**：字典可以包含元组等嵌套对象，例如 `pat['name'][2]` 先按键取出姓名元组，再取出其中的第三个元素。每一层索引都由当前对象的类型决定。
>
> **11. 文件**：`open('myfile.txt', 'w')` 创建文本文件，`write()` 写入内容，`close()` 关闭文件；再次用默认的读取模式打开后可以调用 `read()`。不带路径的文件名会在当前工作目录创建文件，Unix 使用 `ls` 查看，Windows 使用 `dir` 查看。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：第二部分答案围绕“对象 + 操作”展开：运算符和方法不是凭名字通用，而是由对象类型、可变性和协议决定。
- **底层实现**：索引和切片最终调用序列对象的下标协议；字典使用哈希表按哈希值定位键；元组解包先构造可迭代结果再按位置绑定；字符串拼接和切片会创建新对象。
- **设计原因**：不可变对象可以安全共享和哈希，可变列表提供原地更新；字典用可哈希键保证定位稳定；不同类型不自动相加则避免隐式转换造成的歧义。
- **实际问题**：选择列表、元组、字典或字符串时要同时考虑访问方式、是否需要修改、键是否可哈希和复制成本；文件处理应使用 `with open(..., encoding='utf-8')`，避免泄漏句柄和依赖默认编码。
- **初学者误区**：切片越界与索引越界行为不同；字典键不是列表位置；`L[:]` 是浅复制而不是深复制；字符串“看起来像字符数组”却不能原地修改。

### 代码分析

```python
L = [1, 2, 3, 4]
L[1:3] = ['x', 'y', 'z']
X, Y = Y, X
D = {(1, 2, 3): 'tuple key'}

with open('myfile.txt', 'w', encoding='utf-8') as file:
    file.write('Hello file world!\n')
```

- 切片赋值通过序列协议替换一段元素，列表长度可以改变；元组交换在求值右侧时先保存两个对象，再按左侧位置写入名称。
- 字典先对元组键求哈希，再在哈希表中存储引用；`with` 会在代码块正常结束或抛出异常时调用文件对象的退出协议关闭资源。

### 中文翻译

> 请参阅第 15 章中的“知识测验：第三部分练习”。
>
> **1. 编写基础循环**：遍历字符串 `hack`，可以对每个字符调用 `ord()` 打印其整数编码，也可以累加编码得到总和。`for` 循环适用于可迭代对象，循环变量每次绑定到下一个元素。
>
> **2. 计算交互结果**：示例把列表、字典和字符串操作放进循环，展示 `while`、`for`、`break` 和 `continue` 的组合。循环结束后的 `else` 只在循环自然结束时执行；如果遇到 `break`，则跳过 `else`。
>
> **3. 条件语句**：`if/elif/else` 可以按条件选择分支。真值测试会把空字符串、空容器、零和 `None` 视为假，其他对象通常视为真；条件表达式适合简单的二选一结果，不应代替复杂控制流。
>
> **4. 迭代与字典**：直接遍历字典默认得到键。若需要排序输出，可用 `sorted(D)` 生成排序后的键列表；`D.keys()` 返回动态视图，不能直接调用列表的 `sort()`，应先用 `list()` 转换。
>
> **5. 程序逻辑替代方案**：同一个“查找 `2 ** X` 是否在列表中”的问题可以用 `while`、`for`、成员测试、列表构造或 `map`/推导式解决。练习的重点不是唯一答案，而是比较控制流、可读性和中间对象的差异。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：第三部分答案练习的是语句控制流，而不是孤立语法；同一结果可以由循环、条件、成员测试和高阶工具实现。
- **底层实现**：`for` 获取迭代器并反复调用 `__next__()`；`while` 每轮重新求值条件；`break` 跳转离开循环，`else` 是否执行由是否发生 `break` 决定；`sorted()` 会建立新列表。
- **设计原因**：Python 提供多种控制流表达能力，同时保留可读的缩进块；循环 `else` 能表达“没有提前找到/退出”的后置逻辑，但需要明确命名和注释以减少误读。
- **实际问题**：先选择直接表达意图的实现，再关注微优化；对大数据集避免不必要的列表物化，使用生成器或早停；用测试覆盖空输入、边界值和未找到分支。
- **初学者误区**：`for` 的 `else` 不是“每次循环都执行”，字典迭代默认不是值，`keys()` 视图也不是列表；`break` 只退出最近的循环。

### 代码分析

```python
for key in sorted(D):
    print(key, '=>', D[key])

for item in items:
    if matches(item):
        break
else:
    print('not found')
```

- `sorted(D)` 先迭代字典键并构造新列表，`for` 再从该列表取值；字典查询平均为常数时间，但排序成本为 O(n log n)。
- `for/else` 的 `else` 与循环的正常耗尽绑定，不与 `if` 对齐；如果代码需要读者反复确认控制流，最好改用显式标志或辅助函数。

### 中文翻译

> 这道题没什么复杂的，但要注意：`print`，也就是你的 `echo` 函数，本质上是一个多态操作，对不同类型的对象都能做出正确处理。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：这一小节中的函数或实验要点是对象协议、参数匹配、控制流或基准测试，具体含义见相邻原文。
- **底层实现**：解释器通过对象协议、函数调用帧、迭代器、生成器或标准库实现执行示例；运行时结果取决于输入和 Python 实现。
- **设计原因**：示例用小程序对比不同写法，目的是让读者观察语义、可读性和成本，而不是规定唯一编码形式。
- **实际问题**：将示例用于项目时应补充输入校验、异常处理、测试和版本说明，并在目标环境重新测量。
- **初学者误区**：示例能运行不代表它适合所有数据规模或版本；不要把输出结果、微基准数字或一种写法误当成普遍规则。

> 3.12 >>> 

### 英文原文

> echo([1, 2, 3])
> [1, 2, 3]
> >>> 
> echo({'edition': 6})
> {'edition': 6}
> 2
> . 
> Arguments
> : 
> Example B-5
>  gives a sample solution. Remember that you
> have 
> to use 
> print
>  to see results in the test calls because a file isn’t the
> same as code typed interactively; Python doesn’t normally echo the
> results of expression statements in files:

> def adder(x, y):
>  return x + y
> print(adder(5, 1.0))
> print(adder('hack', 'code'))
> print(adder(['a', 'b'], ['c', 'd']))
> And the output:
> 
> $ 
> python3 adder1.py

### 中文翻译

> 这里有两个 `adder` 的备选实现。难点在于怎样把累加器初始化成与传入参数类型匹配的空值：第一个方案靠手工类型测试来判断整数和序列；第二个则用第一个参数初始化，再遍历后面的参数，思路有点像第18章里的 `min` 变体。第二个方案通常更好，但它们都默认所有参数同类型，而且都不能处理字典。

### 深度理解

- **核心概念**：本小节的重点是把原文示例的行为与函数、参数、对象或性能概念对应起来。
- **底层实现**：解释器按原文涉及的对象协议和控制流执行代码，结果会受输入、实现和版本影响。
- **设计原因**：示例用于比较替代写法，不代表作者要求所有项目采用同一种实现。

> : Two alternative 
> adder
>  functions are shown in
> Example B-6
> . The hard part here is figuring out how to initialize 
> an
> accumulator to an empty value of whatever type is passed in. The first
> solution uses manual type testing to look for an integer and an empty
> slice of the first argument (assumed to be a sequence) if the argument is
> determined not to be an integer. The second solution uses the first
> argument to initialize and scan items 2 and beyond, much like one of
> the 
> min
>  function variants shown in 
> Chapter 18
> .
> The second solution may be better. Both of these assume all arguments
> are of the same type, and neither works on dictionaries (as we saw in
> Part II
> , 
> +
>  doesn’t work on mixed types or dictionaries). You could add a
> type test and special code using 
> for
> , 
> update
> , 
> **
> , or 
> |
>  to support
> dictionaries combos, too, but that’s extra credit; see solutions 5 and 6
> ahead for related notes. And yes, there is a 
> sum(
> iterable
> )
>  built-in in
> Python that would make this even simpler, but the point here is to write
> code of your own; you’ll have to eventually:
> Example B-6. 
> Part4/adder2.py
> def adder1(*args):
>  print('adder1:', end=' ')
>  if type(args[0]) == type(0): 
> # Integer?
>  sum = 0 
> # Init to zero
>  else: 
> # else sequence:
>  sum = args[0][:0] 
> # Use empty slice of arg1
>  for arg in args:
>  sum = sum + arg
>  return sum
> def adder2(*args):
>  print('adder2:', end=' ')
> 
>  sum = args[0] 
> # Init to arg1
>  for next in args[1:]:
>  sum += next 
> # Add items 2..N
>  return sum
> for func in (adder1, adder2):
>  print(func(2, 3, 4))
>  print(func('hack', 'code', 'well'))
>  print(func(['a', 'b'], ['c', 'd'], ['e', 'f']))

> adder2: 9

> Example B-8
>  gives the second part’s solution and its output. To iterate
> over keyword arguments, use the 
> **args
>  form in the function header
> and use a loop (e.g., 
> for x in args.keys(): use args[x]
> ), or use
> args.values()
>  to make this the same as summing 
> *args
>  positionals in
> exercise number 3:
> Example B-8. 
> Part4/adder4.py
> def adder1(*args): 
> # Sum any number of positional args
>  tot = args[0] 
> # Same as #3, for comparison and reuse
>  for arg in args[1:]:
>  tot += arg
>  return tot
> def adder2(**args): 
> # Sum any number of keyword args
>  argskeys = list(args.keys()) 
> # list required to index!
>  tot = args[argskeys[0]]
>  for key in argskeys[1:]:
>  tot += args[key]
>  return tot
> def adder3(**args): 
> # Same, but convert to list of values
>  args = list(args.values()) 
> # list needed to index!
>  tot = args[0]
>  for arg in args[1:]:
>  tot += arg
>  return tot
> def adder4(**args): 
> # Same, but reuse positional version
>  return adder1(*args.values())
>

> Dictionary tools
> : Solutions for 
> exercises 5 and 6 are combined
> and listed in 
> Example B-9
> . These are just coding exercises because
> Python now provides dictionary methods 
> D
> .copy()
>  and
> D1
> .update(
> D2
> )
>  to handle things like copying and adding (merging)
> dictionaries. In fact, there are 
> four
>  ways to merge dictionaries today, as
> hinted in solution 3: 
> for
>  loops like those here, 
> D1
> .update(
> D2
> )
> ,
> {*
> *D1
> ,**
> D2
> }
> , and 
> D1
> |
> D2
> . See 
> Chapter 8
>  for more info on and examples
> of these tools. 
> X
> [:]
>  doesn’t work for dictionaries, as they’re not
> sequences (see 
> Chapter 8
>  for details). Also, remember that if you assign
> (
> e = d
> ) rather than copying, you generate a reference to a 
> shared
> dictionary object; changing 
> d
>  changes 
> e
> , too:
> Example B-9. 
> Part4/dicttools.py
> def copyDict(old):
>  new = {}
>  for key in old.keys():
>  new[key] = old[key]
>  return new
> def addDict(d1, d2):
>  new = {}
>  for key in d1.keys():
>  new[key] = d1[key]
>  for key in d2.keys():
>  new[key] = d2[key]
>  return new
> 
> Here is the expected behavior of this code demoed in a REPL:

> >>> 
> e
> {1: 1, 2: 2}
> >>> 
> x = {1: 1}
> >>> 
> y = {2: 2}
> >>> 
> z = addDict(x, y)
> >>> 
> z
> {1: 1, 2: 2}
